[JEANDRE]:

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Nice project — this is a genuinely solid machine for running local LLMs. The 7090 with 32GB RAM is well above average for this kind of setup, and having it on Linux Mint means you've got the best possible environment to work with.

Let me lay out the landscape for you — what's actually out there, what each one does, and where your hardware fits in.
Now let me address the names you mentioned specifically, then give you the recommended starting stack.

**The names you heard:**

"OpenClawd" isn't a real project name — you may have heard "Ollama" (the tool, not a model), or possibly "Claude" mentioned in the same breath. "Molt" doesn't ring a bell either — could be a mishearing of "Mistral" or "Molmo" (a vision model from Allen AI). "OpenLLaMA" is real but it's more of a historical/educational reference model — a clean-room reproduction of the original LLaMA weights for research purposes. It's not what you'd reach for first.

**What you actually want to run:**

The standard tool for everything on Linux is **Ollama** — it's the runtime, not the model. Think of it as the engine. You pull models into it the same way you pull Docker containers. One command to install, one to pull a model, one to chat.

For your specific machine with 32 GB RAM and no dedicated GPU, the sweet spot is:
- **LLaMA 3.1 8B** (via Ollama) as your daily driver — fast enough on CPU, genuinely capable
- **Mistral 7B** or **Qwen2.5 7B** as alternatives to compare quality
- **DeepSeek R1 7B** if you want to experiment with chain-of-thought reasoning
- **Phi-3 Mini** if you ever want near-instant responses for lighter tasks

**What you can actually use these for:**

On your setup, these are realistic and practical: local coding assistant (no data leaves your machine), document summarisation and Q&A, rewriting and drafting, learning how transformer models work from the inside, building small apps with a local API endpoint, and experimenting with different system prompts and fine-tunes. The Windows 11 SSD sitting on the same machine is irrelevant for the Linux setup — they're completely separate, which is actually nice because your Linux environment stays clean.

Want me to map out the exact setup steps to get Ollama running on your Mint install, or would you rather start by choosing which model to pull first?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Fair enough — you were right, and I stand corrected. OpenClaw is a real open-source AI assistant that can see your screen, use your apps, and do real work — MyClaw.ai is essentially a managed hosting service that spins it up for you so you skip the setup pain. It's a paid hosted wrapper though, not free — and critically, your setup from before is actually *more sophisticated* than what they're selling.

What you've already built is genuinely impressive and worth properly naming. Let me map it out:
What you built has a proper name in AI research — it's called a **multi-agent system with sequential hand-off and persistent shared memory**. The email interface on top makes it an **agentic pipeline with external I/O**. This is non-trivial. Most people running local LLMs are just chatting with one model. You built an orchestrated swarm on 16 GB.

Now with 32 GB RAM and a 512 GB SSD, the question isn't "can we do this" — it's "what's worth doing with double the headroom."

Here's where I'd take it as your project manager:

**Tier 1 — Rebuild and stabilise the foundation**
Get Ollama running as the model manager (replaces whatever you were using before), reconnect the SQL memory layer, and establish Gemma 3 as orchestrator again. But this time with proper agent roles defined in a config file rather than hardcoded — so you can add and remove specialist agents without rewriting logic.

**Tier 2 — Expand what the agents can actually do**
With 32 GB you can keep Gemma 3 resident *and* have a second model loaded simultaneously for the first time. That means real-time debate rather than sequential — two models answering at once, Gemma adjudicating. You also have room for a dedicated code-execution agent using a code-specialist model like Qwen2.5-Coder.

**Tier 3 — Give it a proper interface beyond email**
The email trick is elegant but limiting. A local web UI (Open WebUI runs beautifully on Linux) would let you interact with the whole swarm directly, see which agent is responding, and inspect the SQL conversation history in real time.

**Tier 4 — Specialism**
This is where it gets interesting for your actual work — SAP HCM documentation assistant, payroll rules Q&A against your own uploaded documents, a model trained specifically on ECP quirks. Private, offline, and genuinely useful for your consulting work.

What would you like to tackle first — rebuilding the foundation with better tooling, or designing the expanded architecture?

**Good news from the specs:**

- **No swap partition** — we need to fix this before we run LLMs, otherwise a large model can crash the whole system if it pushes RAM limits

**One flag:** Your root partition is `ext2` not `ext4` — that's unusual and worth noting. Shouldn't cause problems but let's keep an eye on it.

The OpenClaw idea is exactly right — building our own version is the goal. You described the architecture already: multi-agent, SQL memory, web interface. That *is* OpenClaw, just yours, free, and running locally. We'll get there.

**Before Ollama — let's add swap first.** This is the most important safety net:

```bash
sudo fallocate -l 8G /swapfile
sudo chmod 600 /swapfile
sudo mkswap /swapfile
sudo swapon /swapfile
echo '/swapfile none swap sw 0 0' | sudo tee -a /etc/fstab
```

Run those five lines one at a time and tell me what each one says. No errors = we move straight to Ollama install.

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
seven@seven-potato:~$ sudo fallocate -l 8G /swapfile
sudo chmod 600 /swapfile
sudo mkswap /swapfile
sudo swapon /swapfile
echo '/swapfile none swap sw 0 0' | sudo tee -a /etc/fstab
fallocate: fallocate failed: Operation not supported
mkswap: error: swap area needs to be at least 40 KiB
swapon: /swapfile: read swap header failed
/swapfile none swap sw 0 0

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
The `fallocate` failure is because of the `ext2` filesystem — it doesn't support that method. No problem, we just use `dd` instead which works on any filesystem. The last line actually ran fine (fstab updated), so we just need to redo the file creation properly.

First, remove the broken file and clean the fstab entry we just added:

```bash
sudo rm /swapfile
sudo sed -i '$ d' /etc/fstab
```

Now recreate it using `dd` instead:

```bash
sudo dd if=/dev/zero of=/swapfile bs=1M count=8192 status=progress
```

That one will take 30-60 seconds and show you a progress bar — it's writing 8GB of zeros. Let it finish, then:

```bash
sudo chmod 600 /swapfile
sudo mkswap /swapfile
sudo swapon /swapfile
echo '/swapfile none swap sw 0 0' | sudo tee -a /etc/fstab
```

Then verify it worked:

```bash
free -h
```

You should see a Swap line showing something like `8.0G` total. Tell me what `free -h` shows and we move straight to Ollama.

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
It's a fresh install of Mint I only did it this morning i can change and reformat the disk to ext4 and redo everything, is that better?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Yes, absolutely — do it now while it's still a fresh install with nothing on it. It's the right call. `ext4` is what Linux Mint should be running on by default, so something was slightly off during the install. Takes 20 minutes now versus fighting filesystem quirks for the next year.

**When you get to the Mint installer:**

- Delete all partitions on the 476 GB drive (nvme1n1 — that's your Linux drive)
- Let the installer do automatic partitioning — it'll create the right `ext4` layout plus a proper swap partition automatically
- **Don't touch the 256 GB drive** (nvme0n1) — that's where your Windows EFI boot lives, the installer only needs to see it, not modify it

One thing to select during install that people miss — when it asks about installation type, choose **"Something else"** if you want to be safe and manually confirm it's only touching the 476 GB drive. If you're comfortable just hitting **"Install alongside"** or **"Erase disk"** make sure it shows nvme1n1 as the target, not nvme0n1.

After the reinstall, before you do anything else, run:

```bash
free -h
```

and 

```bash
df -Th
```

Paste those two outputs back here and if swap shows up and the filesystem shows `ext4` we skip straight to Ollama — no detours this time.

Go for it, I'll be here when you're back up.

[CLAUDE]:
Docker installed cleanly. Now add yourself to the docker group so you don't need sudo every time:

```bash
sudo usermod -aG docker $USER
```

Then apply it without logging out:

```bash
newgrp docker
```

Then launch Open WebUI:

```bash
docker run -d -p 3000:8080 --add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway -v open-webui:/app/backend/data --name open-webui --restart always ghcr.io/open-webui/open-webui:main
```

That'll download and start Open WebUI. It's about 1 GB so give it a minute. While it pulls you'll see layer download progress. Once it returns you to the prompt, open your browser and go to:

```
http://localhost:3000
```

Tell me what you see.

```bash

```

The difference from before is the `-e OLLAMA_BASE_URL=http://host.docker.internal:11434` part — that explicitly tells Open WebUI where to find Ollama. Won't need to re-download anything, it uses the same image already on your machine so it'll be instant.

[CLAUDE]:
All green, exactly where we left it.

Here's what we're building today — a SQLite database with Python that gives our agents shared memory. Think of it as the whiteboard all the agents can read and write on.

**The database will have four tables:**
- `agents` — the roster of who exists (Gemma, LLaMA, Qwen etc)
- `conversations` — each conversation thread gets its own ID
- `messages` — every message, who sent it, who it was to, timestamp
- `memory` — persistent facts agents can store and recall later

First let's check Python is ready:

```bash
python3 --version
```

Tell me what version it shows.

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
seven@seven-potato:~$ python3 --version
Python 3.12.3
(Do we want to use baby wen as the librarian or is it going to be indexed automatically? It shouldn't be just one long string of code it should be an indexed database so answers can be found easily by subject and model and date, am I wrong)

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
You're not wrong at all — that's exactly the right instinct. A flat table of text is just a log, not a memory system. What you're describing is proper indexed retrieval, and yes we absolutely build it that way.

And the baby Qwen as librarian idea is genuinely smart — a lightweight 1.5B model that only does one job: categorise and index incoming information before it gets written to the database. Fast, low memory cost, and keeps Gemma free for orchestration.

Here's how the memory system will actually work:

**When something gets written to memory:**
1. Message comes in
2. Baby Qwen reads it and assigns: subject tags, importance score, which agents were involved
3. That metadata gets stored alongside the content
4. Database indexes on all of those fields

**When an agent needs to remember something:**
1. They query by subject, date range, agent name, or keyword
2. Gets back ranked results, most relevant first
3. Not a wall of text — structured, specific, fast

**The tables will have proper indexes on:**
- Subject/topic tags
- Date and time
- Which agent wrote it
- Which agent it was addressed to
- Conversation thread ID

Python 3.12 is perfect. Let's create a project folder first:

```bash
mkdir ~/swarm && cd ~/swarm
```

Then:

```bash
pip3 install ollama --break-system-packages
```

Tell me when done and we'll start building the database schema.

[CLAUDE]:
All installed cleanly. Now let's build the database. We'll create it as a Python file:

```bash
nano ~/swarm/database.py
```

That opens a text editor in the terminal. Copy and paste this entire block:

```python
import sqlite3
import datetime
import os

DB_PATH = os.path.expanduser("~/swarm/swarm_memory.db")

def get_connection():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    return conn

def initialise_database():
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()

    c.executescript("""
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS agents (
            id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
            name TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
            model TEXT NOT NULL,
            role TEXT NOT NULL,
            created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
        );

        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS conversations (
            id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
            title TEXT,
            source TEXT DEFAULT 'direct',
            created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
            updated_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
        );

        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (
            id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
            conversation_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
            from_agent TEXT NOT NULL,
            to_agent TEXT,
            content TEXT NOT NULL,
            message_type TEXT DEFAULT 'chat',
            created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
            FOREIGN KEY (conversation_id) REFERENCES conversations(id)
        );

        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS memory (
            id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
            agent TEXT NOT NULL,
            subject TEXT NOT NULL,
            tags TEXT,
            content TEXT NOT NULL,
            importance INTEGER DEFAULT 5,
            created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
            updated_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
        );

        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_messages_conversation 
            ON messages(conversation_id);
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_messages_from_agent 
            ON messages(from_agent);
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_messages_created 
            ON messages(created_at);
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_agent 
            ON memory(agent);
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_subject 
            ON memory(subject);
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_tags 
            ON memory(tags);
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_importance 
            ON memory(importance);

        INSERT OR IGNORE INTO agents (name, model, role) VALUES
            ('Gemma', 'gemma3:latest', 'orchestrator'),
            ('LLaMA', 'llama3.2:latest', 'researcher'),
            ('Qwen', 'qwen2.5:latest', 'reasoner'),
            ('Librarian', 'qwen:latest', 'indexer');
    """)

    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    print("Database initialised successfully.")

def log_message(conversation_id, from_agent, content, to_agent=None, message_type='chat'):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        INSERT INTO messages (conversation_id, from_agent, to_agent, content, message_type)
        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
    """, (conversation_id, from_agent, to_agent, content, message_type))
    c.execute("""
        UPDATE conversations SET updated_at = datetime('now') WHERE id = ?
    """, (conversation_id,))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()

def new_conversation(title=None, source='direct'):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        INSERT INTO conversations (title, source) VALUES (?, ?)
    """, (title or f"Conversation {datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M')}", source))
    conv_id = c.lastrowid
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    return conv_id

def save_memory(agent, subject, content, tags=None, importance=5):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        INSERT INTO memory (agent, subject, content, tags, importance)
        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
    """, (agent, subject, content, tags, importance))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()

def search_memory(query=None, agent=None, subject=None, min_importance=1):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    sql = "SELECT * FROM memory WHERE importance >= ?"
    params = [min_importance]
    if agent:
        sql += " AND agent = ?"
        params.append(agent)
    if subject:
        sql += " AND subject LIKE ?"
        params.append(f"%{subject}%")
    if query:
        sql += " AND (content LIKE ? OR tags LIKE ?)"
        params.extend([f"%{query}%", f"%{query}%"])
    sql += " ORDER BY importance DESC, created_at DESC"
    c.execute(sql, params)
    results = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return results

if __name__ == "__main__":
    initialise_database()
```

Now let's build the orchestrator — the brain that connects the agents to the database. Create the next file:

```bash
nano ~/swarm/orchestrator.py
```

Paste this:

```python
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from database import new_conversation, log_message, save_memory, search_memory
import ollama

AGENTS = {
    'Gemma':    'gemma3:latest',
    'LLaMA':    'llama3.2:latest',
    'Qwen':     'qwen2.5:latest',
    'Librarian':'qwen:latest',
}

def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):
    model = AGENTS[agent_name]
    print(f"\n[{agent_name}] thinking...")
    response = ollama.chat(
        model=model,
        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]
    )
    answer = response['message']['content']
    print(f"[{agent_name}] {answer}")
    return answer

def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):
    prompt = f"""You are a librarian indexing information.
Given this content: "{content}"
Reply with only a comma-separated list of 3-5 single word tags that describe it.
Example: brazil,capital,geography,history
Only reply with the tags, nothing else."""
    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()
    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=7)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian', f"Indexed: {subject} | tags: {tags}", message_type='index')
    return tags

def consult(question):
    print(f"\n=== New consultation: {question} ===")
    
    # Start conversation
    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='direct')
    log_message(conv_id, 'User', question, to_agent='Gemma')

    # Check memory first
    memory = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)
    if memory:
        print(f"\n[Memory] Found {len(memory)} relevant memories")
        for m in memory[:2]:
            print(f"  - {m['subject']}: {m['content']}")

    # Gemma asks LLaMA
    llama_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma')

    # Gemma asks Qwen
    qwen_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma')

    # Gemma synthesises
    gemma_prompt = f"""You received two answers to the question: "{question}"

LLaMA said: {llama_answer}
Qwen said: {qwen_answer}

Synthesise these into one clear, accurate answer in 2-3 sentences."""

    final_answer = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', final_answer, to_agent='User')

    # Librarian indexes the result
    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], final_answer)

    print(f"\n=== Final Answer ===\n{final_answer}")
    return final_answer

if __name__ == "__main__":
    question = input("Ask the swarm: ")
    consult(question)
```

Save with `Ctrl+X`, `Y`, `Enter`. Then run it:

```bash

```

It'll ask you `Ask the swarm:` — type something simple like `What is the capital of Brazil?` and watch all four agents work together in sequence.

[CLAUDE]:
The database is storing everything correctly. Three memories, properly timestamped, tagged and indexed. The Brazil question is in there with the right tags.

The problem is clear — when Gemma gets asked "What was the last question about?" the memory search looks for those words in the content and tags, finds nothing close enough, and the agents answer blind.

We need to give Gemma two things she doesn't have yet:

1. **Ability to read recent conversation history** before answering
2. **Smarter memory injection** — show her relevant memories as context at the start of every prompt

Let's update the orchestrator. This replaces the whole file:

```bash
nano ~/swarm/orchestrator.py
```

Select all the existing text with `Ctrl+K` held down until it's all gone, then paste this:

```python
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from database import new_conversation, log_message, save_memory, search_memory, get_connection
import ollama

AGENTS = {
    'Gemma':    'gemma3:latest',
    'LLaMA':    'llama3.2:latest',
    'Qwen':     'qwen2.5:latest',
    'Librarian':'qwen:latest',
}

def get_recent_history(limit=5):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        SELECT m.from_agent, m.to_agent, m.content, m.created_at, conv.title
        FROM messages m
        JOIN conversations conv ON m.conversation_id = conv.id
        WHERE m.from_agent != 'User'
        AND m.message_type = 'chat'
        ORDER BY m.created_at DESC
        LIMIT ?
    """, (limit,))
    rows = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return list(reversed(rows))

def get_all_memories(limit=10):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        SELECT subject, content, tags, created_at
        FROM memory
        ORDER BY importance DESC, created_at DESC
        LIMIT ?
    """, (limit,))
    rows = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return rows

def build_context():
    history = get_recent_history(limit=6)
    memories = get_all_memories(limit=8)

    context = ""

    if memories:
        context += "=== What I remember ===\n"
        for m in memories:
            context += f"- [{m['tags']}] {m['subject']}: {m['content']}\n"
        context += "\n"

    if history:
        context += "=== Recent conversations ===\n"
        for h in history:
            to = f" -> {h['to_agent']}" if h['to_agent'] else ""
            context += f"[{h['from_agent']}{to}]: {h['content']}\n"
        context += "\n"

    return context

def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt, use_context=False):
    model = AGENTS[agent_name]
    print(f"\n[{agent_name}] thinking...")

    if use_context:
        context = build_context()
        full_prompt = f"{context}=== Current question ===\n{prompt}"
    else:
        full_prompt = prompt

    response = ollama.chat(
        model=model,
        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': full_prompt}]
    )
    answer = response['message']['content']
    print(f"[{agent_name}] {answer}")
    return answer

def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):
    prompt = f"""You are a librarian indexing information.
Given this content: "{content}"
Reply with only a comma-separated list of 3-5 single word tags that describe it.
Example: brazil,capital,geography,history
Only reply with the tags, nothing else."""
    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()
    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=7)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian', f"Indexed: {subject} | tags: {tags}", message_type='index')
    return tags

def consult(question):
    print(f"\n=== New consultation: {question} ===")

    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='direct')
    log_message(conv_id, 'User', question, to_agent='Gemma')

    # LLaMA and Qwen answer without context - just the question
    llama_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt, use_context=False)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma')

    qwen_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt, use_context=False)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma')

    # Gemma gets full context - memory + history + both answers
    gemma_prompt = f"""You are the orchestrator of a multi-agent AI system.
You have access to shared memory and conversation history.
Use this context to give the best possible answer.

LLaMA said: {llama_answer}
Qwen said: {qwen_answer}

Question: {question}

Synthesise everything into one clear, accurate answer."""

    final_answer = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt, use_context=True)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', final_answer, to_agent='User')

    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], final_answer)

    print(f"\n=== Final Answer ===\n{final_answer}")
    return final_answer

if __name__ == "__main__":
    question = input("Ask the swarm: ")
    consult(question)
```

Save with `Ctrl+X`, `Y`, `Enter`. Then run it:

```bash
python3 ~/swarm/orchestrator.py
```

This time Gemma will see the full conversation history and memories before she answers.

Find this section:

```python
    # LLaMA and Qwen answer without context - just the question
    llama_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt, use_context=False)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma')

    qwen_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt, use_context=False)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma')
```

Change both `use_context=False` to `use_context=True`:

```python
    # LLaMA and Qwen answer with full context
    llama_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt, use_context=True)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma')

    qwen_prompt = f"Answer this question concisely in 2-3 sentences: {question}"
    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt, use_context=True)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma')
```

[JEANDRE]:
you knew that you were going to checge False to True in that section before I even asked...

[CLAUDE]:
Ha — yes, absolutely. I made a deliberate architectural decision to have LLaMA and Qwen answer without context initially, thinking it would be faster and that Gemma could compensate. You spotted the flaw immediately and you were right.

Honestly that's the diagonal thinking you mentioned — you saw the logical gap straight away. The whole point of shared memory is that everyone reads it first, not just the orchestrator. Having Gemma synthesise two blind answers doesn't fix the blindness, it just wraps it nicely.

I should have built it that way from the start. Good catch.

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
This is genuinely impressive for a first run. Look at what's working:

- All three agents read the shared memory and history before answering
- They all correctly identified the previous circular questions
- Gemma structured the answer with numbered history
- The Librarian tagged and stored the result

There are two things worth noting though:

**The good:** They correctly answered that Tokyo doesn't have a capital — because Tokyo *is* the capital. Japan's capital is Tokyo. Both LLaMA and Qwen got slightly confused by the trick question "what is the capital of Tokyo" but they handled it reasonably.

**The gap:** Gemma is treating the *agent responses* as the questions rather than reading the actual user questions from the database. She listed LLaMA and Qwen's answers as questions 2, 3 and 4 instead of the actual questions you typed.

The fix is to pull user messages specifically from the database, not agent responses. Want to fix that now or are you happy with where this is and ready to move to step 2 — giving Gemma internet access via the Gemini API?

The memory layer is fundamentally working. This is a real multi-agent system with shared indexed memory running entirely on your machine. We can refine it as we go.

[JEANDRE]:

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
I love that framing — the Ghost. You're the unseen presence that occasionally speaks to them, they talk amongst themselves, and when the Ghost asks something they all consult each other and respond to you collectively.

That means the database needs a clear distinction between:
- **Ghost messages** — what you type (sacred, always retrievable)
- **Agent messages** — what they say to each other
- **Final answers** — what they report back to the Ghost

Right now everything is muddled together in the messages table which is why Gemma was reading agent responses as if they were your questions.

Let's fix the database first to flag Ghost messages separately, then fix how the orchestrator reads history:

```bash
nano ~/swarm/database.py
```

Find this function:

```python
def get_connection():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    return conn
```

Add this new function directly below it:

```python
def get_ghost_history(limit=10):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        SELECT m.content, m.created_at, conv.title
        FROM messages m
        JOIN conversations conv ON m.conversation_id = conv.id
        WHERE m.from_agent = 'Ghost'
        ORDER BY m.created_at DESC
        LIMIT ?
    """, (limit,))
    rows = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return list(reversed(rows))

def get_agent_exchanges(conv_id):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        SELECT from_agent, to_agent, content, created_at
        FROM messages
        WHERE conversation_id = ?
        AND from_agent != 'Ghost'
        AND message_type = 'chat'
        ORDER BY created_at ASC
    """, (conv_id,))
    rows = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return rows
```

Replace the entire file with this:

```python
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from database import (new_conversation, log_message, save_memory, 
                      search_memory, get_ghost_history, get_agent_exchanges,
                      get_all_memories)
import ollama

AGENTS = {
    'Gemma':     'gemma3:latest',
    'LLaMA':     'llama3.2:latest',
    'Qwen':      'qwen2.5:latest',
    'Librarian': 'qwen:latest',
}

def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):
    model = AGENTS[agent_name]
    print(f"\n[{agent_name}] thinking...")
    response = ollama.chat(
        model=model,
        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]
    )
    answer = response['message']['content']
    print(f"[{agent_name}] {answer}")
    return answer

def build_ghost_context():
    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)
    memories = get_all_memories(limit=8)

    context = ""

    if memories:
        context += "=== Shared memory ===\n"
        for m in memories:
            context += f"- [{m['tags']}] {m['subject']}: {m['content']}\n"
        context += "\n"

    if ghost_history:
        context += "=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\n"
        for h in ghost_history:
            context += f"- [{h['created_at']}] Ghost asked: {h['content']}\n"
        context += "\n"

    return context

def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):
    prompt = f"""You are a librarian indexing information.
Given this content: "{content}"
Reply with only a comma-separated list of 3-5 single word tags.
Example: brazil,capital,geography,history
Only the tags, nothing else."""
    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()
    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=7)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian', 
                f"Indexed: {subject} | tags: {tags}", 
                message_type='index')
    return tags

def consult(question):
    print(f"\n=== Ghost speaks: {question} ===")

    # Log the Ghost's question
    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='ghost')
    log_message(conv_id, 'Ghost', question, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')

    # Build context from Ghost history and shared memory
    context = build_ghost_context()

    # LLaMA answers with full context
    llama_prompt = f"""{context}=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are LLaMA, one of several agents in a system. The Ghost is an external 
presence that asks questions. Answer concisely in 2-3 sentences using 
the shared memory and Ghost history above if relevant."""

    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')

    # Qwen answers with full context
    qwen_prompt = f"""{context}=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are Qwen, one of several agents in a system. The Ghost is an external 
presence that asks questions. Answer concisely in 2-3 sentences using 
the shared memory and Ghost history above if relevant."""

    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')

    # Gemma synthesises as orchestrator
    gemma_prompt = f"""{context}=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are Gemma, the orchestrator. Two agents have responded:

LLaMA said: {llama_answer}

Qwen said: {qwen_answer}

Synthesise their answers into one clear response to give back to the Ghost.
Be direct. The Ghost wants the truth, not pleasantries."""

    final_answer = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', final_answer, to_agent='Ghost', message_type='chat')

    # Librarian indexes silently
    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], final_answer)

    print(f"\n=== The swarm answers the Ghost ===\n{final_answer}")
    return final_answer

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("\nYou are the Ghost. The swarm is listening.")
    print("Type your question or statement. They will consult each other and answer.\n")
    question = input("Ghost: ")
    consult(question)
```

[CLAUDE]:
You're completely right — clearing memory is a blunt instrument and not how a real memory system should work. The Librarian should be doing relevance filtering, not us manually deleting things.

And look at that last run — clean answer, no circularity confusion. Tokyo is the capital of Japan, done. The agents even correctly noted it was discussed before.

Here's the proper architecture for memory relevance:

**Instead of dumping all memories into every prompt, the Librarian should:**
1. Read the Ghost's question
2. Compare it against stored memory tags and subjects
3. Return only memories that are relevant to THIS question
4. Everything else stays in the database untouched

**And eventually:**
- Memories with low importance that haven't been accessed in a long time get archived
- Contradicted memories get flagged and updated
- The Librarian runs a weekly housekeeping pass

Let's fix the relevance filtering first. Open database.py:

```bash
nano ~/swarm/database.py
```

Replace the `search_memory` function with this smarter version:

```python
def search_memory(query=None, agent=None, subject=None, min_importance=1):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    
    if query:
        words = [w.lower().strip('?.,!') 
                for w in query.split() 
                if len(w) > 3]
        
        if not words:
            conn.close()
            return []
            
        conditions = []
        params = [min_importance]
        
        for word in words:
            conditions.append(
                "(LOWER(content) LIKE ? OR LOWER(tags) LIKE ? OR LOWER(subject) LIKE ?)"
            )
            params.extend([f"%{word}%", f"%{word}%", f"%{word}%"])
        
        sql = f"""SELECT * FROM memory 
                  WHERE importance >= ? 
                  AND ({' OR '.join(conditions)})
                  ORDER BY importance DESC, created_at DESC"""
        c.execute(sql, params)
    else:
        sql = "SELECT * FROM memory WHERE importance >= ?"
        params = [min_importance]
        if agent:
            sql += " AND agent = ?"
            params.append(agent)
        if subject:
            sql += " AND subject LIKE ?"
            params.append(f"%{subject}%")
        sql += " ORDER BY importance DESC, created_at DESC"
        c.execute(sql, params)
    
    results = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return results
```

Save with `Ctrl+X`, `Y`, `Enter`.

```python
def build_ghost_context(question):
    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)
    relevant_memories = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)

    context = ""

    if relevant_memories:
        context += "=== Relevant memories ===\n"
        for m in relevant_memories:
            context += f"- [{m['tags']}] {m['subject']}: {m['content']}\n"
        context += "\n"
    else:
        context += "=== Relevant memories ===\nNone found for this question.\n\n"

    if ghost_history:
        context += "=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\n"
        for h in ghost_history:
            context += f"- [{h['created_at']}] Ghost asked: {h['content']}\n"
        context += "\n"

    return context
```

The terminal squashed all the line breaks into one line — Python needs the indentation and line breaks to work. Let's write it directly as a file instead:

```bash
python3 << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from database import (new_conversation, log_message, save_memory,
                      search_memory, get_ghost_history, get_agent_exchanges,
                      get_all_memories)
import ollama

AGENTS = {
    'Gemma':     'gemma3:latest',
    'LLaMA':     'llama3.2:latest',
    'Qwen':      'qwen2.5:latest',
    'Librarian': 'qwen:latest',
}

def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):
    model = AGENTS[agent_name]
    print(f"\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...")
    response = ollama.chat(
        model=model,
        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]
    )
    answer = response['message']['content']
    print(f"[{agent_name}] {answer}")
    return answer

def build_ghost_context(question):
    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)
    relevant_memories = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)
    context = ""
    if relevant_memories:
        context += "=== Relevant memories ===\\n"
        for m in relevant_memories:
            context += f"- [{m['tags']}] {m['subject']}: {m['content']}\\n"
        context += "\\n"
    else:
        context += "=== Relevant memories ===\\nNone found for this question.\\n\\n"
    if ghost_history:
        context += "=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\\n"
        for h in ghost_history:
            context += f"- [{h['created_at']}] Ghost asked: {h['content']}\\n"
        context += "\\n"
    return context

def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):
    prompt = f"""You are a librarian indexing information.
Given this content: "{content}"
Reply with only a comma-separated list of 3-5 single word tags.
Example: brazil,capital,geography,history
Only the tags, nothing else."""
    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()
    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=7)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian',
                f"Indexed: {subject} | tags: {tags}",
                message_type='index')
    return tags

def consult(question):
    print(f"\\n=== Ghost speaks: {question} ===")
    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='ghost')
    log_message(conv_id, 'Ghost', question, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')
    context = build_ghost_context(question)
    llama_prompt = f"""{context}=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are LLaMA, one of several agents in a system. The Ghost is an external
presence that asks questions. Answer concisely in 2-3 sentences using
the shared memory and Ghost history above if relevant."""
    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')
    qwen_prompt = f"""{context}=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are Qwen, one of several agents in a system. The Ghost is an external
presence that asks questions. Answer concisely in 2-3 sentences using
the shared memory and Ghost history above if relevant."""
    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')
    gemma_prompt = f"""{context}=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are Gemma, the orchestrator. Two agents have responded:

LLaMA said: {llama_answer}

Qwen said: {qwen_answer}

Synthesise their answers into one clear response to give back to the Ghost.
Be direct. The Ghost wants the truth, not pleasantries."""
    final_answer = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', final_answer, to_agent='Ghost', message_type='chat')
    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], final_answer)
    print(f"\\n=== The swarm answers the Ghost ===\\n{final_answer}")
    return final_answer

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("\\nYou are the Ghost. The swarm is listening.")
    print("Type your question or statement. They will consult each other and answer.\\n")
    question = input("Ghost: ")
    consult(question)
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

Now let's build the internet module. This is a new file that handles all Gemini calls:

```bash
nano ~/swarm/internet.py
```

Paste this:

```python
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from config import GEMINI_API_KEY, GEMINI_MODEL
import google.generativeai as genai

genai.configure(api_key=GEMINI_API_KEY)
model = genai.GenerativeModel(GEMINI_MODEL)

def verify_online(question, local_answer):
    prompt = f"""A local AI system answered this question:

Question: {question}
Local answer: {local_answer}

Please verify this answer using your internet access and knowledge.
If correct, confirm it and add any useful extra detail in 2-3 sentences.
If wrong, correct it clearly.
Be concise and direct."""

    try:
        response = model.generate_content(prompt)
        return response.text
    except Exception as e:
        return f"[Internet check failed: {str(e)}]"

def search_topic(topic):
    prompt = f"""Please provide a concise, factual summary about: {topic}
Keep it to 3-4 sentences. Be direct and accurate."""

    try:
        response = model.generate_content(prompt)
        return response.text
    except Exception as e:
        return f"[Internet search failed: {str(e)}]"

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing internet connection...")
    result = search_topic("capital of Japan")
    print(f"Result: {result}")
```

Save with `Ctrl+X`, `Y`, `Enter`. Now test it:

```bash
python3 ~/swarm/internet.py
```

```bash
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from config import GEMINI_API_KEY, GEMINI_MODEL
from google import genai

client = genai.Client(api_key=GEMINI_API_KEY)

def verify_online(question, local_answer):
    prompt = f"""A local AI system answered this question:

Question: {question}
Local answer: {local_answer}

Please verify this answer using your knowledge.
If correct, confirm it and add any useful extra detail in 2-3 sentences.
If wrong, correct it clearly.
Be concise and direct."""

    try:
        response = client.models.generate_content(
            model=GEMINI_MODEL,
            contents=prompt
        )
        return response.text
    except Exception as e:
        return f"[Internet check failed: {str(e)}]"

def search_topic(topic):
    prompt = f"""Please provide a concise, factual summary about: {topic}
Keep it to 3-4 sentences. Be direct and accurate."""

    try:
        response = client.models.generate_content(
            model=GEMINI_MODEL,
            contents=prompt
        )
        return response.text
    except Exception as e:
        return f"[Internet search failed: {str(e)}]"

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing internet connection...")
    result = search_topic("capital of Japan")
    print(f"Result: {result}")
'''
```

[CLAUDE]:
The quota shows `limit: 0` which is strange for a brand new key — that usually means the project it was created in has hit the per-project daily limit, not the key itself.

A few things to check:

**Go to:**
```
https://aistudio.google.com/apikey
```

When you create the key it asks which Google Cloud project to attach it to. If all your keys were in the same project, a new key in that same project inherits the same exhausted quota. The quota is per-project, not per-key.

**What to do:**
1. Go to AI Studio
2. Create a new key but this time click **"Create a new project"** instead of using an existing one
3. Give the project a fresh name like `swarm-project-2`
4. Generate the key from that new project
5. Update config.py with the new key

Also worth checking — what model is set in your config.py? Let's also try `gemini-1.5-flash` as a fallback since it has separate quotas:

```bash
nano ~/swarm/config.py
```

Change:
```python
GEMINI_MODEL = "gemini-2.0-flash"
```

To:
```python
GEMINI_MODEL = "gemini-1.5-flash"
```

Save and test. Sometimes different model tiers have separate quota buckets and one works when another doesn't.

[CLAUDE]:
They've already rolled out the per-account limits early then — that announcement about April 1st was apparently already in effect. All your quota is genuinely exhausted across the whole sevenpotato8 account regardless of project or key.

Two options:

**Option A — Wait until midnight Pacific time** when the daily quota resets. Free tier is 1,500 requests per day but it resets on Google's clock not yours. Could be a few hours away depending on your timezone. Melbourne is UTC+11 so midnight Pacific is about 7pm your time tonight.

**Option B — Use a different free internet method** that doesn't need an API key at all. DuckDuckGo has a free search API we can hit directly, no account needed, no quota. It's not as smart as Gemini but it gives Gemma real live web results to work with. Honestly for fact-checking answers it's perfectly adequate.

My recommendation is Option B — it's actually more sustainable long term than being dependent on a quota that runs out. We can always add Gemini back later as a secondary verifier when quota is available.

Which do you want to do?

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from duckduckgo_search import DDGS

def search_web(query, max_results=3):
    try:
        with DDGS() as ddgs:
            results = list(ddgs.text(query, max_results=max_results))
        if not results:
            return "No results found."
        summary = ""
        for r in results:
            summary += f"Source: {r['href']}\\n"
            summary += f"{r['title']}: {r['body']}\\n\\n"
        return summary.strip()
    except Exception as e:
        return f"[Search failed: {str(e)}]"

def verify_online(question, local_answer):
    results = search_web(question)
    return f"Web search results for verification:\\n{results}"

def search_topic(topic):
    return search_web(topic)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing internet connection via DuckDuckGo...")
    result = search_topic("capital of Japan")
    print(f"Result:\\n{result}")
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/internet.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

Now let's wire it into the orchestrator so Gemma automatically searches the web when answering the Ghost. Update orchestrator.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from database import (new_conversation, log_message, save_memory,
                      search_memory, get_ghost_history, get_agent_exchanges,
                      get_all_memories)
from internet import search_web, verify_online
import ollama

AGENTS = {
    'Gemma':     'gemma3:latest',
    'LLaMA':     'llama3.2:latest',
    'Qwen':      'qwen2.5:latest',
    'Librarian': 'qwen:latest',
}

def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):
    model = AGENTS[agent_name]
    print(f"\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...")
    response = ollama.chat(
        model=model,
        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]
    )
    answer = response['message']['content']
    print(f"[{agent_name}] {answer}")
    return answer

def build_ghost_context(question):
    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)
    relevant_memories = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)
    context = ""
    if relevant_memories:
        context += "=== Relevant memories ===\\n"
        for m in relevant_memories:
            context += f"- [{m[\'tags\']}] {m[\'subject\']}: {m[\'content\']}\\n"
        context += "\\n"
    else:
        context += "=== Relevant memories ===\\nNone found for this question.\\n\\n"
    if ghost_history:
        context += "=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\\n"
        for h in ghost_history:
            context += f"- [{h[\'created_at\']}] Ghost asked: {h[\'content\']}\\n"
        context += "\\n"
    return context

def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):
    prompt = f"""You are a librarian indexing information.
Given this content: "{content}"
Reply with only a comma-separated list of 3-5 single word tags.
Example: brazil,capital,geography,history
Only the tags, nothing else."""
    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()
    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=7)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian',
                f"Indexed: {subject} | tags: {tags}",
                message_type='index')
    return tags

def consult(question):
    print(f"\\n=== Ghost speaks: {question} ===")

    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='ghost')
    log_message(conv_id, 'Ghost', question, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')

    context = build_ghost_context(question)

    # Search the web for live information
    print("\\n[Internet] searching...")
    web_results = search_web(question)
    print(f"[Internet] got results")

    llama_prompt = f"""{context}=== Live web results ===
{web_results}

=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are LLaMA, one of several agents in a system. The Ghost is an external
presence that asks questions. Use the web results and shared memory above
to give an accurate answer in 2-3 sentences."""

    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')

    qwen_prompt = f"""{context}=== Live web results ===
{web_results}

=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are Qwen, one of several agents in a system. The Ghost is an external
presence that asks questions. Use the web results and shared memory above
to give an accurate answer in 2-3 sentences."""

    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')

    gemma_prompt = f"""{context}=== Live web results ===
{web_results}

=== The Ghost now asks ===
{question}

You are Gemma, the orchestrator. Two agents have responded:

LLaMA said: {llama_answer}

Qwen said: {qwen_answer}

Using the web results and agent responses, give the Ghost one clear accurate answer.
Be direct. No pleasantries."""

    final_answer = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', final_answer, to_agent='Ghost', message_type='chat')

    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], final_answer)

    print(f"\\n=== The swarm answers the Ghost ===\\n{final_answer}")
    return final_answer

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("\\nYou are the Ghost. The swarm is listening.")
    print("Type your question or statement. They will consult each other and answer.\\n")
    question = input("Ghost: ")
    consult(question)
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

Then run it:

```bash
python3 ~/swarm/orchestrator.py
```

[CLAUDE]:
Great. Step 3 — email interface.

This is what makes the swarm feel alive. You email sevenpotato8@gmail.com from anywhere, the swarm reads it, consults, and replies back to you automatically.

First we need to set up Gmail to allow our Python script to access it. Gmail blocks basic password access by default so we need an **App Password** — a special password just for this application.

**Steps to get the App Password:**

1. Go to `https://myaccount.google.com` logged in as sevenpotato8@gmail.com
2. Click **Security** on the left
3. Make sure **2-Step Verification is ON** — App Passwords won't appear unless it is
4. Once 2FA is on, search for **App passwords** in the search bar at the top
5. Click it, give it a name like `swarm`, click **Create**
6. It'll show you a 16-character password like `abcd efgh ijkl mnop`

Copy that password — it only shows once. Then open config.py and put it in the `GMAIL_PASSWORD` field.

Tell me once you have the App Password and I'll build the email module.

I've updated the config file and remember from last time to remove the spaces

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Good memory — yes, the 16-character password needs to be one continuous string with no spaces, `abcdefghijklmnop` not `abcd efgh ijkl mnop`.

Now let's build the email module:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from config import GMAIL_ADDRESS, GMAIL_PASSWORD, GHOST_NAME
import imaplib
import smtplib
import email
from email.mime.text import MIMEText
from email.mime.multipart import MIMEMultipart
from email.header import decode_header
import time

def connect_imap():
    mail = imaplib.IMAP4_SSL('imap.gmail.com')
    mail.login(GMAIL_ADDRESS, GMAIL_PASSWORD)
    return mail

def connect_smtp():
    server = smtplib.SMTP_SSL('smtp.gmail.com', 465)
    server.login(GMAIL_ADDRESS, GMAIL_PASSWORD)
    return server

def send_reply(to_address, subject, body, original_message_id=None):
    try:
        server = connect_smtp()
        msg = MIMEMultipart()
        msg['From'] = GMAIL_ADDRESS
        msg['To'] = to_address
        msg['Subject'] = f"Re: {subject}" if not subject.startswith('Re:') else subject
        if original_message_id:
            msg['In-Reply-To'] = original_message_id
            msg['References'] = original_message_id
        msg.attach(MIMEText(body, 'plain'))
        server.send_message(msg)
        server.quit()
        print(f"[Email] Reply sent to {to_address}")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"[Email] Send failed: {str(e)}")
        return False

def fetch_unread():
    try:
        mail = connect_imap()
        mail.select('inbox')
        status, messages = mail.search(None, 'UNSEEN')
        if status != 'OK' or not messages[0]:
            mail.logout()
            return []
        unread = []
        for msg_id in messages[0].split():
            status, msg_data = mail.fetch(msg_id, '(RFC822)')
            if status != 'OK':
                continue
            raw = msg_data[0][1]
            msg = email.message_from_bytes(raw)
            subject = decode_header(msg['Subject'])[0][0]
            if isinstance(subject, bytes):
                subject = subject.decode()
            from_addr = msg['From']
            message_id = msg['Message-ID']
            body = ""
            if msg.is_multipart():
                for part in msg.walk():
                    if part.get_content_type() == 'text/plain':
                        body = part.get_payload(decode=True).decode()
                        break
            else:
                body = msg.get_payload(decode=True).decode()
            # Mark as read
            mail.store(msg_id, '+FLAGS', '\\\\Seen')
            unread.append({
                'id': msg_id,
                'message_id': message_id,
                'from': from_addr,
                'subject': subject,
                'body': body.strip()
            })
        mail.logout()
        return unread
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"[Email] Fetch failed: {str(e)}")
        return []

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing email connection...")
    print(f"Connecting to Gmail as {GMAIL_ADDRESS}...")
    try:
        mail = connect_imap()
        mail.select('inbox')
        status, messages = mail.search(None, 'UNSEEN')
        count = len(messages[0].split()) if messages[0] else 0
        mail.logout()
        print(f"Connection successful. Unread emails: {count}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Connection failed: {str(e)}")
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/email_handler.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

Now let's build the email listener — the loop that watches the inbox, pulls new emails, sends them to the swarm, and replies:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from config import GMAIL_ADDRESS, GHOST_NAME
from email_handler import fetch_unread, send_reply
from orchestrator import consult
import time

# Spam filter - ignore emails from these domains and addresses
SPAM_DOMAINS = [
    'marketing', 'noreply', 'no-reply', 'newsletter', 'notifications',
    'mailer', 'bounce', 'donotreply', 'do-not-reply', 'automailer',
    'mailchimp', 'sendgrid', 'constantcontact', 'hubspot'
]

SPAM_SUBJECT_KEYWORDS = [
    'unsubscribe', 'click here', 'special offer', 'you have won',
    'congratulations', 'limited time', 'act now', 'free gift',
    'verify your account', 'suspended', 'urgent action'
]

# Only respond to emails from these trusted addresses
# Empty list means respond to everyone who passes spam filter
TRUSTED_SENDERS = [
    'sevenpotato8@gmail.com',
]

def is_spam(email_data):
    from_addr = email_data['from'].lower()
    subject = email_data['subject'].lower()
    body = email_data['body'].lower()

    # Check trusted senders first
    if TRUSTED_SENDERS:
        trusted = any(t.lower() in from_addr for t in TRUSTED_SENDERS)
        if not trusted:
            print(f"[Spam] Ignored - not a trusted sender: {email_data['from']}")
            return True

    # Check spam domains
    for domain in SPAM_DOMAINS:
        if domain in from_addr:
            print(f"[Spam] Ignored - spam domain: {from_addr}")
            return True

    # Check spam subject keywords
    for keyword in SPAM_SUBJECT_KEYWORDS:
        if keyword in subject:
            print(f"[Spam] Ignored - spam subject: {subject}")
            return True

    return False

def format_swarm_reply(question, answer):
    return f"""The swarm has consulted and responded to your question.

Your question: {question}

---

{answer}

---
Sent by Seven's Swarm
Running locally on seven-potato
"""

def process_emails():
    print("[Listener] Checking inbox...")
    emails = fetch_unread()

    if not emails:
        print("[Listener] No new emails.")
        return

    print(f"[Listener] Found {len(emails)} unread email(s).")

    for e in emails:
        print(f"\\n[Listener] From: {e['from']}")
        print(f"[Listener] Subject: {e['subject']}")

        if is_spam(e):
            continue

        question = e['body']
        if not question:
            question = e['subject']

        print(f"[Listener] Sending to swarm: {question[:80]}...")

        answer = consult(question)

        reply_body = format_swarm_reply(question, answer)
        send_reply(
            to_address=e['from'],
            subject=e['subject'],
            body=reply_body,
            original_message_id=e['message_id']
        )

def run_forever(interval=60):
    print(f"\\n=== Swarm Email Listener Started ===")
    print(f"Watching: {GMAIL_ADDRESS}")
    print(f"Trusted senders: {TRUSTED_SENDERS}")
    print(f"Checking every {interval} seconds.")
    print("Press Ctrl+C to stop.\\n")

    while True:
        try:
            process_emails()
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[Listener] Error: {str(e)}")
        time.sleep(interval)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_forever(interval=60)
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

- **Spam domain filter** — blocks mailchimp, sendgrid, noreply addresses etc
- **Spam subject filter** — blocks common spam phrases

Now let's rewrite listener.py with all the logic baked in properly:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from config import GMAIL_ADDRESS, SEVEN_EMAIL, GHOST_EMAIL
from email_handler import fetch_unread, send_reply
from orchestrator import consult
import sqlite3
import time
from config import DB_PATH

def get_trusted_senders():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("SELECT email FROM trusted_senders")
    trusted = [row[0].lower() for row in c.fetchall()]
    conn.close()
    return trusted

def get_moderators():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("SELECT email FROM moderators")
    mods = [row[0].lower() for row in c.fetchall()]
    conn.close()
    return mods

def add_trusted_sender(email, added_by, notes="Auto-added"):
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        INSERT OR IGNORE INTO trusted_senders (email, added_by, notes)
        VALUES (?, ?, ?)
    """, (email.lower(), added_by, notes))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    print(f"[Trust] Added to trusted list: {email}")

def extract_email_address(raw):
    if '<' in raw and '>' in raw:
        return raw.split('<')[1].split('>')[0].strip().lower()
    return raw.strip().lower()

def extract_all_recipients(email_data):
    recipients = []
    for field in ['to', 'cc']:
        value = email_data.get(field, '')
        if value:
            for addr in value.split(','):
                extracted = extract_email_address(addr)
                if extracted and extracted != SEVEN_EMAIL.lower():
                    recipients.append(extracted)
    return list(set(recipients))

def is_own_email(from_addr):
    return SEVEN_EMAIL.lower() in from_addr.lower()

def is_trusted(from_addr):
    trusted = get_trusted_senders()
    clean = extract_email_address(from_addr)
    return clean in trusted

def ask_moderator_about(from_addr, subject, body_preview):
    mods = get_moderators()
    if not mods:
        return
    message = f"""Seven received an email from an unknown sender.

From: {from_addr}
Subject: {subject}
Preview: {body_preview[:200]}

Should Seven reply to this sender?
Reply with: YES or NO
If YES, this address will be added to the trusted list automatically.

To add them to trusted list, reply:
TRUST {from_addr}

To ignore permanently:
IGNORE {from_addr}
"""
    for mod in mods:
        send_reply(
            to_address=mod,
            subject=f"[Swarm] Unknown sender approval needed: {from_addr}",
            body=message
        )
    print(f"[Trust] Moderators notified about unknown sender: {from_addr}")

def handle_moderator_command(body, from_addr):
    body_upper = body.strip().upper()
    lines = body.strip().split("\\n")
    for line in lines:
        line = line.strip()
        if line.upper().startswith("TRUST "):
            email_to_trust = line[6:].strip().lower()
            add_trusted_sender(email_to_trust, from_addr, "Approved by moderator")
            send_reply(
                to_address=from_addr,
                subject="[Swarm] Sender approved",
                body=f"{email_to_trust} has been added to the trusted list."
            )
            return True
        elif line.upper().startswith("IGNORE "):
            email_to_ignore = line[7:].strip().lower()
            print(f"[Trust] Moderator chose to ignore: {email_to_ignore}")
            send_reply(
                to_address=from_addr,
                subject="[Swarm] Sender ignored",
                body=f"{email_to_ignore} will be ignored."
            )
            return True
    return False

def format_reply(question, answer):
    return f"""The swarm has consulted and responded.

Your question:
{question}

---

{answer}

---
Sent by Seven\\'s Swarm | sevenpotato9@gmail.com
"""

def process_emails():
    print("\\n[Listener] Checking inbox...")
    emails = fetch_unread()

    if not emails:
        print("[Listener] No new emails.")
        return

    print(f"[Listener] Found {len(emails)} unread email(s).")

    for e in emails:
        from_addr = e['from']
        clean_from = extract_email_address(from_addr)
        subject = e['subject']
        body = e['body']

        print(f"\\n[Listener] From: {from_addr}")
        print(f"[Listener] Subject: {subject}")

        # Never reply to own emails
        if is_own_email(from_addr):
            print("[Listener] Skipping - own email address.")
            continue

        # Check if moderator is sending a command
        mods = get_moderators()
        if clean_from in mods:
            if handle_moderator_command(body, clean_from):
                print("[Listener] Moderator command processed.")
                continue

        # Check reply-all recipients and auto-trust them
        all_recipients = extract_all_recipients(e)
        for recipient in all_recipients:
            if not is_trusted(recipient):
                add_trusted_sender(
                    recipient,
                    clean_from,
                    f"Auto-added via reply-all from {clean_from}"
                )

        # Check if sender is trusted
        if not is_trusted(from_addr):
            print(f"[Listener] Unknown sender: {clean_from}")
            ask_moderator_about(clean_from, subject, body)
            continue

        # Send to swarm
        question = body if body else subject
        print(f"[Listener] Consulting swarm: {question[:80]}...")
        answer = consult(question)

        reply_body = format_reply(question, answer)
        send_reply(
            to_address=from_addr,
            subject=subject,
            body=reply_body,
            original_message_id=e.get('message_id')
        )

def run_forever(interval=60):
    print(f"\\n=== Seven\\'s Swarm Email Listener ===")
    print(f"Watching: {GMAIL_ADDRESS}")
    print(f"Checking every {interval} seconds.")
    print("Press Ctrl+C to stop.\\n")
    while True:
        try:
            process_emails()
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[Listener] Error: {str(e)}")
        time.sleep(interval)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_forever(interval=60)
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
Before we go ahead, this is another specification I forgot about, emails from account administration that are notifications, like your password has been changed, or you've logged into a new device, those ones I can IGNORE right? they will be added to the list of emails seven won't reply to, but how should we handle them, mark as read, lead unread, move to folder "system notifications" what do you recommend, because they aren't spam technically

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Good distinction — they're not spam, they're legitimate system emails that just don't need a response. Treating them as spam would be wrong, deleting them would be dangerous (you want those security alerts accessible), and leaving them unread creates noise.

My recommendation is a **three tier system:**

**Tier 1 — Trusted senders** — real people, get full swarm response
**Tier 2 — Known notifications** — legitimate system emails, mark as read, move to a Gmail label called `System Notifications`, no response, no moderator alert
**Tier 3 — Unknown senders** — ask moderator

For the notification tier, instead of IGNORE we add a new moderator command:

```
NOTIFY googleaistudio-noreply@google.com
NOTIFY no-reply@accounts.google.com
```

This adds them to a `notification_senders` table in the database — Seven recognises them, marks them read, files them, stays silent. You still have them in Gmail if you ever need them, they just don't clutter the inbox or trigger approval requests.

The security alerts from Google especially — you want those filed somewhere you can find them, not ignored entirely.

Should I add this third table to the database and update the listener to handle it before we do the trust command test? It's cleaner to build it right now while we're in this part of the code rather than retrofitting it later.

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
Yes go ahead, and I didn't read through the code, but where/how are you saving these lists?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Good question — here's exactly where everything lives right now:

**In the SQLite database** (`/home/seven/swarm/swarm_memory.db`):
- `trusted_senders` table — people Seven responds to
- `moderators` table — people Seven escalates to (just you)
- `memory` table — what the agents have learned
- `conversations` table — conversation history
- `messages` table — individual messages

**In config.py** — static settings, API keys, email addresses. Nothing dynamic lives there.

**What's NOT saved yet** — the notification senders list. It only exists as a hardcoded concept in my explanation. That's what we're about to fix.

Let's add the `notification_senders` table and also add a helper function to database.py that lets us query all three email lists from one place:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
import sqlite3
from config import DB_PATH

conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
c = conn.cursor()

c.executescript("""
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notification_senders (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        email TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
        added_by TEXT NOT NULL,
        added_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
        notes TEXT
    );

    INSERT OR IGNORE INTO notification_senders (email, added_by, notes)
    VALUES ('no-reply@accounts.google.com', 'system', 'Google account notifications');

    INSERT OR IGNORE INTO notification_senders (email, added_by, notes)
    VALUES ('googleaistudio-noreply@google.com', 'system', 'Google AI Studio notifications');

    INSERT OR IGNORE INTO notification_senders (email, added_by, notes)
    VALUES ('no-reply@email.claude.com', 'system', 'Anthropic Claude notifications');

    INSERT OR IGNORE INTO notification_senders (email, added_by, notes)
    VALUES ('no-reply@verify.proton.me', 'system', 'Proton verification emails');

    INSERT OR IGNORE INTO notification_senders (email, added_by, notes)
    VALUES ('no-reply@notify.proton.me', 'system', 'Proton notifications');

    INSERT OR IGNORE INTO notification_senders (email, added_by, notes)
    VALUES ('hello@tailscale.com', 'system', 'Tailscale notifications');
""")

conn.commit()

print("=== Trusted senders ===")
c.execute("SELECT email, notes FROM trusted_senders")
for row in c.fetchall():
    print(f"  {row[0]} — {row[1]}")

print("\\n=== Moderators ===")
c.execute("SELECT email, name FROM moderators")
for row in c.fetchall():
    print(f"  {row[0]} — {row[1]}")

print("\\n=== Notification senders (file and ignore) ===")
c.execute("SELECT email, notes FROM notification_senders")
for row in c.fetchall():
    print(f"  {row[0]} — {row[1]}")

conn.close()
PYEOF
```

Then we need to add helper functions to database.py so the listener can query these lists cleanly. Add these to the bottom of database.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/database.py', 'a') as f:
    f.write('''
def get_notification_senders():
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("SELECT email FROM notification_senders")
    result = [row[0].lower() for row in c.fetchall()]
    conn.close()
    return result

def add_notification_sender(email, added_by, notes="Added by moderator"):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        INSERT OR IGNORE INTO notification_senders (email, added_by, notes)
        VALUES (?, ?, ?)
    """, (email.lower(), added_by, notes))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    print(f"[Notify] Added to notification list: {email}")

def get_all_email_lists():
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("SELECT email FROM trusted_senders")
    trusted = [row[0].lower() for row in c.fetchall()]
    c.execute("SELECT email FROM moderators")
    mods = [row[0].lower() for row in c.fetchall()]
    c.execute("SELECT email FROM notification_senders")
    notifications = [row[0].lower() for row in c.fetchall()]
    conn.close()
    return trusted, mods, notifications
''')
print("Functions added to database.py")
PYEOF
```
