[CLAUDE]:
All three lists are in the database and the helper functions are in database.py. Clean.

Now let's rewrite listener.py with the full three-tier system plus Gmail label filing for notifications:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from config import GMAIL_ADDRESS, SEVEN_EMAIL, GHOST_EMAIL
from email_handler import fetch_unread, send_reply
from database import (get_all_email_lists, add_trusted_sender, 
                      add_notification_sender)
from orchestrator import consult
import sqlite3
import imaplib
import time
from config import DB_PATH, GMAIL_PASSWORD

def get_imap_connection():
    mail = imaplib.IMAP4_SSL('imap.gmail.com')
    mail.login(GMAIL_ADDRESS, GMAIL_PASSWORD)
    return mail

def ensure_gmail_label(mail, label):
    mail.create(label)

def move_to_label(mail, msg_id, label):
    try:
        ensure_gmail_label(mail, label)
        mail.copy(msg_id, label)
        mail.store(msg_id, '+FLAGS', '\\\\Deleted')
        mail.expunge()
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"[Label] Could not move email: {str(e)}")

def extract_email_address(raw):
    if '<' in raw and '>' in raw:
        return raw.split('<')[1].split('>')[0].strip().lower()
    return raw.strip().lower()

def extract_all_recipients(email_data):
    recipients = []
    for field in ['to', 'cc']:
        value = email_data.get(field, '')
        if value:
            for addr in value.split(','):
                extracted = extract_email_address(addr)
                if extracted and extracted != SEVEN_EMAIL.lower():
                    recipients.append(extracted)
    return list(set(recipients))

def is_own_email(from_addr):
    return SEVEN_EMAIL.lower() in from_addr.lower()

def classify_sender(from_addr):
    trusted, mods, notifications = get_all_email_lists()
    clean = extract_email_address(from_addr)
    if clean == SEVEN_EMAIL.lower():
        return 'self'
    if clean in mods:
        return 'moderator'
    if clean in trusted:
        return 'trusted'
    if clean in notifications:
        return 'notification'
    return 'unknown'

def handle_moderator_command(body, from_addr):
    lines = body.strip().split('\\n')
    for line in lines:
        line = line.strip()
        if line.upper().startswith('TRUST '):
            email_to_trust = line[6:].strip().lower()
            add_trusted_sender(email_to_trust, from_addr, 
                             'Approved by moderator')
            send_reply(
                to_address=from_addr,
                subject='[Swarm] Sender approved',
                body=f'{email_to_trust} has been added to the trusted list.'
            )
            return True
        elif line.upper().startswith('NOTIFY '):
            email_to_notify = line[7:].strip().lower()
            add_notification_sender(email_to_notify, from_addr,
                                  'Filed as notification by moderator')
            send_reply(
                to_address=from_addr,
                subject='[Swarm] Sender added to notifications',
                body=f'{email_to_notify} will be filed silently as a notification.'
            )
            return True
        elif line.upper().startswith('IGNORE '):
            email_to_ignore = line[7:].strip().lower()
            add_notification_sender(email_to_ignore, from_addr,
                                  'Ignored by moderator')
            send_reply(
                to_address=from_addr,
                subject='[Swarm] Sender ignored',
                body=f'{email_to_ignore} will be silently ignored from now on.'
            )
            return True
    return False

def ask_moderator_about(from_addr, subject, body_preview):
    trusted, mods, notifications = get_all_email_lists()
    if not mods:
        return
    message = f"""Seven received an email from an unknown sender.

From: {from_addr}
Subject: {subject}
Preview: {body_preview[:200]}

Reply with one of these commands:

TRUST {from_addr}
  → Add to trusted list, Seven will respond to future emails

NOTIFY {from_addr}
  → File silently as a system notification, no response ever

IGNORE {from_addr}
  → Same as NOTIFY but labelled as ignored
"""
    for mod in mods:
        send_reply(
            to_address=mod,
            subject=f'[Swarm] Unknown sender: {from_addr}',
            body=message
        )
    print(f'[Trust] Moderator notified about: {from_addr}')

def format_reply(question, answer):
    return f"""The swarm has consulted and responded.

Your question:
{question}

---

{answer}

---
Sent by Seven\\'s Swarm | sevenpotato9@gmail.com
"""

def process_emails():
    print('\\n[Listener] Checking inbox...')
    emails = fetch_unread()

    if not emails:
        print('[Listener] No new emails.')
        return

    print(f'[Listener] Found {len(emails)} unread email(s).')

    # Track unknowns we already notified about this pass
    already_notified = set()

    for e in emails:
        from_addr = e['from']
        clean_from = extract_email_address(from_addr)
        subject = e['subject']
        body = e['body']
        msg_id = e['id']

        print(f'\\n[Listener] From: {from_addr}')
        print(f'[Listener] Subject: {subject}')

        classification = classify_sender(from_addr)
        print(f'[Listener] Classification: {classification}')

        # Never reply to own emails
        if classification == 'self':
            print('[Listener] Skipping - own email.')
            continue

        # System notifications - file silently
        if classification == 'notification':
            print('[Listener] Filing as system notification.')
            try:
                mail = get_imap_connection()
                mail.select('inbox')
                ensure_gmail_label(mail, 'System Notifications')
                mail.copy(msg_id, 'System Notifications')
                mail.store(msg_id, '+FLAGS', '\\\\Seen')
                mail.logout()
                print('[Listener] Filed and marked as read.')
            except Exception as ex:
                print(f'[Listener] Could not file: {str(ex)}')
            continue

        # Moderator commands
        if classification == 'moderator':
            if handle_moderator_command(body, clean_from):
                print('[Listener] Moderator command processed.')
                continue

        # Unknown sender
        if classification == 'unknown':
            if clean_from not in already_notified:
                ask_moderator_about(clean_from, subject, body)
                already_notified.add(clean_from)
            else:
                print(f'[Listener] Already notified moderator about {clean_from} this pass.')
            continue

        # Trusted sender - check reply-all recipients and auto-trust
        all_recipients = extract_all_recipients(e)
        for recipient in all_recipients:
            trusted, mods, notifications = get_all_email_lists()
            if (recipient not in trusted and 
                recipient not in mods and 
                recipient not in notifications):
                add_trusted_sender(
                    recipient, clean_from,
                    f'Auto-added via reply-all from {clean_from}'
                )

        # Send to swarm
        question = body if body else subject
        print(f'[Listener] Consulting swarm: {question[:80]}...')
        answer = consult(question)

        reply_body = format_reply(question, answer)
        send_reply(
            to_address=from_addr,
            subject=subject,
            body=reply_body,
            original_message_id=e.get('message_id')
        )

def run_forever(interval=60):
    print(f'\\n=== Seven\\'s Swarm Email Listener ===')
    print(f'Watching: {GMAIL_ADDRESS}')
    print(f'Checking every {interval} seconds.')
    print('Press Ctrl+C to stop.\\n')
    while True:
        try:
            process_emails()
        except Exception as e:
            print(f'[Listener] Error: {str(e)}')
        time.sleep(interval)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    run_forever(interval=60)
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

[CLAUDE]:
There's a UTF-8 encoding error — one of the emails has special characters that Python's default decoder can't handle. Easy fix, we just tell it to be more lenient with encoding. Fix the email_handler.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/email_handler.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

content = content.replace(
    "body = part.get_payload(decode=True).decode()",
    "body = part.get_payload(decode=True).decode('utf-8', errors='replace')"
).replace(
    "body = msg.get_payload(decode=True).decode()",
    "body = msg.get_payload(decode=True).decode('utf-8', errors='replace')"
)

with open('/home/seven/swarm/email_handler.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("Fixed.")
PYEOF
```

Let's check directly what's in the trusted list right now:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'

[JEANDRE]:
Yes lets build the debate system as the last one for today

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Perfect. The debate system is where it gets really interesting — instead of agents just answering independently and Gemma synthesising, they actually challenge each other's answers.

Here's how it'll work:

**Round 1** — LLaMA and Qwen answer the question independently
**Round 2** — Each reads the other's answer and challenges or agrees with it
**Round 3** — Gemma reads the debate, makes a ruling, delivers the verdict

It's like a courtroom. Gemma is the judge, LLaMA and Qwen are advocates, the Ghost is the client.

Let's build it as a separate file so we can choose between normal consultation and full debate mode:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = '''import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
from database import (new_conversation, log_message, save_memory,
                      search_memory, get_ghost_history, get_all_memories)
from internet import search_web
import ollama

AGENTS = {
    'Gemma':     'gemma3:latest',
    'LLaMA':     'llama3.2:latest',
    'Qwen':      'qwen2.5:latest',
    'Librarian': 'qwen:latest',
}

def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):
    model = AGENTS[agent_name]
    print(f"\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...")
    response = ollama.chat(
        model=model,
        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]
    )
    answer = response['message']['content']
    print(f"[{agent_name}] {answer}")
    return answer

def build_context(question):
    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)
    relevant_memories = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)
    context = ""
    if relevant_memories:
        context += "=== Relevant memories ===\\n"
        for m in relevant_memories:
            context += f"- [{m[\\'tags\\']}] {m[\\'subject\\']}: {m[\\'content\\']}\\n"
        context += "\\n"
    if ghost_history:
        context += "=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\\n"
        for h in ghost_history:
            context += f"- [{h[\\'created_at\\']}] Ghost asked: {h[\\'content\\']}\\n"
        context += "\\n"
    return context

def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):
    prompt = f"""You are a librarian indexing information.
Given this content: "{content}"
Reply with only a comma-separated list of 3-5 single word tags.
Only the tags, nothing else."""
    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()
    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=8)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian',
                f"Indexed: {subject} | tags: {tags}",
                message_type='index')
    return tags

def debate(question):
    print(f"\\n=== Ghost calls a debate: {question} ===")

    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='debate')
    log_message(conv_id, 'Ghost', question, to_agent='Gemma', 
                message_type='chat')

    context = build_context(question)

    print("\\n[Internet] searching...")
    web_results = search_web(question)
    print("[Internet] got results")

    # Round 1 - Independent answers
    print("\\n--- Round 1: Independent answers ---")

    llama_prompt = f"""{context}=== Live web results ===
{web_results}

=== The Ghost asks ===
{question}

You are LLaMA. Give your best answer in 3-4 sentences.
Be confident and specific. State facts, not hedges."""

    llama_r1 = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_r1, 
                message_type='debate_r1')

    qwen_prompt = f"""{context}=== Live web results ===
{web_results}

=== The Ghost asks ===
{question}

You are Qwen. Give your best answer in 3-4 sentences.
Be confident and specific. State facts, not hedges."""

    qwen_r1 = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_r1, 
                message_type='debate_r1')

    # Round 2 - Challenge each other
    print("\\n--- Round 2: Challenge ---")

    llama_challenge_prompt = f"""You are LLaMA in a debate.

You said: {llama_r1}

Qwen said: {qwen_r1}

Do you agree or disagree with Qwen?
If you disagree, state specifically what is wrong and why.
If you agree, add something Qwen missed.
Be direct and concise. 2-3 sentences only."""

    llama_r2 = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_challenge_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_r2, 
                message_type='debate_r2')

    qwen_challenge_prompt = f"""You are Qwen in a debate.

You said: {qwen_r1}

LLaMA said: {llama_r1}

Do you agree or disagree with LLaMA?
If you disagree, state specifically what is wrong and why.
If you agree, add something LLaMA missed.
Be direct and concise. 2-3 sentences only."""

    qwen_r2 = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_challenge_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_r2, 
                message_type='debate_r2')

    # Round 3 - Gemma judges
    print("\\n--- Round 3: Gemma rules ---")

    gemma_prompt = f"""{context}=== Live web results ===
{web_results}

=== The Ghost asked ===
{question}

=== The debate ===

LLaMA round 1: {llama_r1}
Qwen round 1: {qwen_r1}

LLaMA challenge: {llama_r2}
Qwen challenge: {qwen_r2}

You are Gemma, the judge. 
Review the debate and the web results.
Decide who made the stronger case or where both were right or wrong.
Deliver one clear final verdict to the Ghost.
Be direct. No pleasantries. 3-4 sentences maximum."""

    verdict = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)
    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', verdict, 
                to_agent='Ghost', message_type='verdict')

    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], verdict)

    print(f"\\n=== Gemma\\'s verdict for the Ghost ===")
    print(f"\\nQuestion: {question}")
    print(f"\\nLLaMA (R1): {llama_r1}")
    print(f"\\nQwen (R1): {qwen_r1}")
    print(f"\\nLLaMA challenges: {llama_r2}")
    print(f"\\nQwen challenges: {qwen_r2}")
    print(f"\\nVerdict: {verdict}")

    return verdict

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("\\nYou are the Ghost. Call a debate.")
    print("The agents will argue, then Gemma will judge.\\n")
    question = input("Ghost calls a debate on: ")
    debate(question)
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/debate.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

Then run it:

```bash
python3 ~/swarm/debate.py
```

Ask it something genuinely debatable — try `Is nuclear energy the best solution to climate change?`

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
Does Llama and Qwen also have internet access? currently they are answering questions blindly without being able to update their own knowledge, for now that is fine but we can add another backlog item for each model to have their own database of updated information based on previous responses, does that make sense? I don't want to blow it out of control but eventually it will be a lot of memories to go through and they would be debating each other only based on shared memory, or am I wrong?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
You're exactly right on all counts. Let me break it down:

**Current state:**
- LLaMA and Qwen get the web results passed to them in their prompt — so they do see internet data, but they didn't fetch it themselves, Gemma's orchestrator fetched it and handed it to them
- All agents share the same memory pool — one database, everyone reads the same thing

**Your concern is valid and it's called "model bias convergence"** — if all agents learn from the same shared memory, over time they'll start agreeing with each other more and more because they're all reading the same past answers. The debate becomes an echo chamber. Two agents who've both read the same memory will naturally converge toward the same conclusions.

**Your instinct about separate databases is the right architectural solution:**

- **LLaMA** — own memory, own browsing history, own knowledge base
- **Qwen** — own memory, own knowledge base  
- **Gemma** — orchestrator memory plus access to read both agent databases when judging
- **Shared pool** — only verified facts that Gemma has ruled on after a debate

That way LLaMA and Qwen genuinely develop different knowledge bases and perspectives over time, making their debates meaningful rather than performative.

**Backlog item: Agent-specific memory pools** — each agent gets their own memory table, Gemma gets a verified facts table fed only by debate verdicts.

For now the shared pool is fine — they still have their own training biases to argue from. Want to run the debate first and see how they go?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Escape character issue with the nested quotes. Let's rewrite it cleanly:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = open('/home/seven/swarm/debate.py', 'r').read()

# Fix the problematic lines
old = "context += f\"- [{m[\\'tags\\']}] {m[\\'subject\\']}]: {m[\\'content\\']}\\n\""
new = "context += \"- [\" + str(m['tags']) + \"] \" + str(m['subject']) + \": \" + str(m['content']) + \"\\n\""

with open('/home/seven/swarm/debate.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

content = content.replace(
    "context += f\"- [{m[\\'tags\\']}] {m[\\'subject\\']}: {m[\\'content\\']}\\n\"",
    "context += \"- [\" + str(m['tags']) + \"] \" + str(m['subject']) + \": \" + str(m['content']) + \"\\n\""
)

Actually — that approach is getting messy. Let me just rewrite the whole file cleanly using a different quoting strategy that avoids the escape issue entirely:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from database import (new_conversation, log_message, save_memory,",
    "                      search_memory, get_ghost_history, get_all_memories)",
    "from internet import search_web",
    "import ollama",
    "",
    "AGENTS = {",
    "    'Gemma':     'gemma3:latest',",
    "    'LLaMA':     'llama3.2:latest',",
    "    'Qwen':      'qwen2.5:latest',",
    "    'Librarian': 'qwen:latest',",
    "}",
    "",
    "def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):",
    "    model = AGENTS[agent_name]",
    "    print(f'\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...')",
    "    response = ollama.chat(",
    "        model=model,",
    "        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]",
    "    )",
    "    answer = response['message']['content']",
    "    print(f'[{agent_name}] {answer}')",
    "    return answer",
    "",
    "def build_context(question):",
    "    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)",
    "    relevant_memories = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)",
    "    context = ''",
    "    if relevant_memories:",
    "        context += '=== Relevant memories ===\\n'",
    "        for m in relevant_memories:",
    "            tags = str(m['tags'])",
    "            subj = str(m['subject'])",
    "            cont = str(m['content'])",
    "            context += '- [' + tags + '] ' + subj + ': ' + cont + '\\n'",
    "        context += '\\n'",
    "    if ghost_history:",
    "        context += '=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\\n'",
    "        for h in ghost_history:",
    "            context += '- [' + str(h['created_at']) + '] Ghost asked: ' + str(h['content']) + '\\n'",
    "        context += '\\n'",
    "    return context",
    "",
    "def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):",
    "    prompt = 'You are a librarian indexing information. Given this content: \"' + content + '\" Reply with only a comma-separated list of 3-5 single word tags. Only the tags, nothing else.'",
    "    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()",
    "    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=8)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian',",
    "                'Indexed: ' + subject + ' | tags: ' + tags,",
    "                message_type='index')",
    "    return tags",
    "",
    "def debate(question):",
    "    print('\\n=== Ghost calls a debate: ' + question + ' ===')",
    "",
    "    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='debate')",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Ghost', question, to_agent='Gemma',",
    "                message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    context = build_context(question)",
    "",
    "    print('\\n[Internet] searching...')",
    "    web_results = search_web(question)",
    "    print('[Internet] got results')",
    "",
    "    # Round 1 - Independent answers",
    "    print('\\n--- Round 1: Independent answers ---')",
    "",
    "    llama_prompt = context + '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\nYou are LLaMA. Give your best answer in 3-4 sentences. Be confident and specific. State facts, not hedges.'",
    "",
    "    llama_r1 = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_r1, message_type='debate_r1')",
    "",
    "    qwen_prompt = context + '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\nYou are Qwen. Give your best answer in 3-4 sentences. Be confident and specific. State facts, not hedges.'",
    "",
    "    qwen_r1 = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_r1, message_type='debate_r1')",
    "",
    "    # Round 2 - Challenge",
    "    print('\\n--- Round 2: Challenge ---')",
    "",
    "    llama_challenge = 'You are LLaMA in a debate.\\n\\nYou said: ' + llama_r1 + '\\n\\nQwen said: ' + qwen_r1 + '\\n\\nDo you agree or disagree with Qwen? If you disagree, state specifically what is wrong and why. If you agree, add something Qwen missed. Be direct. 2-3 sentences only.'",
    "",
    "    llama_r2 = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_challenge)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_r2, message_type='debate_r2')",
    "",
    "    qwen_challenge = 'You are Qwen in a debate.\\n\\nYou said: ' + qwen_r1 + '\\n\\nLLaMA said: ' + llama_r1 + '\\n\\nDo you agree or disagree with LLaMA? If you disagree, state specifically what is wrong and why. If you agree, add something LLaMA missed. Be direct. 2-3 sentences only.'",
    "",
    "    qwen_r2 = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_challenge)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_r2, message_type='debate_r2')",
    "",
    "    # Round 3 - Gemma judges",
    "    print('\\n--- Round 3: Gemma rules ---')",
    "",
    "    gemma_prompt = context + '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n=== The Ghost asked ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\n=== The debate ===\\n\\nLLaMA round 1: ' + llama_r1 + '\\nQwen round 1: ' + qwen_r1 + '\\n\\nLLaMA challenge: ' + llama_r2 + '\\nQwen challenge: ' + qwen_r2 + '\\n\\nYou are Gemma, the judge. Review the debate and the web results. Decide who made the stronger case or where both were right or wrong. Deliver one clear final verdict to the Ghost. Be direct. No pleasantries. 3-4 sentences maximum.'",
    "",
    "    verdict = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', verdict,",
    "                to_agent='Ghost', message_type='verdict')",
    "",
    "    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], verdict)",
    "",
    "    print('\\n=== Gemma verdict for the Ghost ===')",
    "    print('\\nQuestion: ' + question)",
    "    print('\\nLLaMA (R1): ' + llama_r1)",
    "    print('\\nQwen (R1): ' + qwen_r1)",
    "    print('\\nLLaMA challenges: ' + llama_r2)",
    "    print('\\nQwen challenges: ' + qwen_r2)",
    "    print('\\nVerdict: ' + verdict)",
    "",
    "    return verdict",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    print('\\nYou are the Ghost. Call a debate.')",
    "    print('The agents will argue, then Gemma will judge.\\n')",
    "    question = input('Ghost calls a debate on: ')",
    "    debate(question)",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/debate.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

Then run it:

python3 - << 'PYEOF'
content = """# Seven's Swarm — Project Documentation
*Last updated: 2026-03-19*

## What is this?
A local multi-agent AI system running entirely on a Dell OptiPlex 7090
with 32GB RAM and Linux Mint. No cloud dependency. No subscription.
The Ghost (Jeandre) interacts with a swarm of local LLMs that consult
each other, share memory, search the web, and communicate via email.

---

## Architecture overview

```
Ghost (Jeandre)
    |
    | email / terminal
    v
Gemma (Orchestrator)
    |
    |-- reads: shared memory, Ghost history, web results
    |-- delegates to: LLaMA, Qwen
    |-- judges: debate verdicts
    |-- commands: Librarian to index
    v
SQLite Database (swarm_memory.db)
    |-- agents
    |-- conversations
    |-- messages
    |-- memory (shared, indexed by Librarian)
    |-- trusted_senders
    |-- moderators
    |-- notification_senders
```

---

## Files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| database.py | All database operations, schema, helper functions |
| orchestrator.py | Standard consultation flow — Ghost asks, agents answer, Gemma synthesises |
| debate.py | Debate flow — R1 independent, R2 challenge, R3 Gemma judges |
| internet.py | Web search via DuckDuckGo |
| email_handler.py | Gmail IMAP/SMTP — fetch unread, send reply |
| listener.py | Email loop — classify senders, route to swarm, handle commands |
| config.py | All secrets and settings — NEVER share this file |
| PROJECT.md | This document |

---

## Models

| Name | Model | Role |
|------|-------|------|
| Gemma | gemma3:latest (4.3B) | Orchestrator, judge |
| LLaMA | llama3.2:latest (3.2B) | Sub-agent, researcher |
| Qwen | qwen2.5:latest (7.6B) | Sub-agent, reasoner |
| Librarian | qwen:latest (1.5B) | Indexer, tagger |

---

## Email access control

Three tiers:

1. **Trusted senders** — get full swarm response
2. **Notification senders** — filed silently to System Notifications label, no response
3. **Unknown senders** — moderator (Jeandre) gets approval request

Moderator commands (reply with these in email body):
- TRUST email@address.com
- NOTIFY email@address.com
- IGNORE email@address.com

Reply-all auto-trust: anyone CC'd into a thread by a trusted sender gets auto-added.

---

- Fresh Linux Mint install (ext4 this time)

## Backlog

### Priority 1 — Core improvements
- [ ] BL-001: Email body stripping — remove quoted reply chains and signatures before sending to swarm
- [ ] BL-002: Pending queue — when unknown sender gets approved, retroactively answer their original question
- [ ] BL-003: Reply formatting — answer should be prominent, not buried after repeated question

### Priority 2 — Memory improvements
- [ ] BL-005: Agent-specific memory pools — LLaMA and Qwen get separate databases to prevent debate echo chamber
- [ ] BL-006: Verified facts table — only Gemma-approved debate verdicts get written here
- [ ] BL-007: Librarian housekeeping — archive old low-importance memories on a schedule
- [ ] BL-008: Memory contradiction detection — flag when new info conflicts with stored memory

### Priority 3 — Agent improvements
- [ ] BL-009: Each agent fetches their own web results independently
- [ ] BL-010: Dedicated email handler agent — Gemma knows email context vs new question vs command
- [ ] BL-011: LLaMA over-referencing fix — stop hallucinating references to previous questions
- [ ] BL-012: Gemma preamble fix — answer directly without saying 'here is a response for the Ghost'

### Priority 4 — Infrastructure
- [ ] BL-013: Auto-start listener on boot as a systemd service
- [ ] BL-014: Windows bootloader repair (EFI overwritten during Mint reinstall)
- [ ] BL-015: Gemini API internet access — replace DuckDuckGo when quota resets
- [ ] BL-016: Web UI integration — debate and consultation accessible from Open WebUI

### Priority 5 — Future vision
- [ ] BL-017: SAP HCM/ECP specialist agent — trained on payroll documentation
- [ ] BL-018: Novel writing assistant — agents help with 'you can only do what you can do when you can do it'
- [ ] BL-019: OpenClaw-style interface — full browser-based agent management UI
- [ ] BL-020: Voice interface — speak to the swarm, get spoken replies

---

## Open questions

- How do we handle time-sensitive memories (e.g. who is PM of Australia changes)?
- Should the Librarian assign importance scores dynamically based on debate outcomes?
- What happens when two agents have conflicting memories from separate pools?
- Should Seven have a personality that persists across sessions?

[CLAUDE]:
The answer IS there — "You are running on a Linux Mint Dell Optiplex 7090 with 32 GB RAM and 512 GB SSD storage" — it's just buried and the formatting is flat because it's all one block of text with no line breaks in the email client.

Two issues:
1. **The question is too long** — it repeats the entire introduction back which is noisy
2. **No line breaks** — the `\\n` characters aren't rendering as actual line breaks in the email

Both are quick fixes. Let's fix the format_reply function in listener.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

old = """def format_reply(question, answer):
    return f\"\"\"The swarm has consulted and responded.

Your question:
{question}

---

{answer}

---
Sent by Seven\\'s Swarm | sevenpotato9@gmail.com
\"\"\""""

new = """def format_reply(question, answer):
    short_q = question[:150] + '...' if len(question) > 150 else question
    return (
        "The swarm has consulted and responded.\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        "Your question:\\r\\n" + short_q + "\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        "---\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        answer + "\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        "---\\r\\n" +
        "Sent by Seven's Swarm | sevenpotato9@gmail.com"
    )"""

content = content.replace(old, new)

with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("Fixed.")
PYEOF
```

The key change is `\\r\\n` instead of `\\n` — email clients expect Windows-style line endings for proper line breaks. Also truncates the question to 150 characters so it doesn't dominate the reply.

Now the most important fix — **auto-start on boot:**

```bash
sudo nano /etc/systemd/system/swarm-listener.service
```

Paste this:

```
[Unit]
Description=Seven's Swarm Email Listener
After=network.target ollama.service

[Service]
Type=simple
User=seven
WorkingDirectory=/home/seven/swarm
ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3 /home/seven/swarm/listener.py
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=10

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target
```

Save with `Ctrl+X`, `Y`, `Enter`. Then enable it:

```bash
sudo systemctl daemon-reload
sudo systemctl enable swarm-listener
sudo systemctl start swarm-listener
sudo systemctl status swarm-listener
```

```bash
cat ~/swarm/PROJECT.md
```

Then we start with BL-001 — email body stripping. The logic is straightforward:

1. Find where the quoted reply starts (usually `---`, `From:`, `On ... wrote:`)
2. Cut everything below that line
3. Strip signature blocks (usually after `--`, `Kind regards`, `Sent from`)
4. Pass only the clean question to the swarm

Tell me what PROJECT.md shows and we'll get into it.

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Perfect — clean documentation. Also note BL-013 is done so we need to mark that off. Let's update PROJECT.md at the end of this session.

Now BL-001 — email body stripping. Let's build a dedicated cleaner module so it's reusable across the whole system:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import re",
    "",
    "# Patterns that indicate the start of a quoted reply chain",
    "QUOTE_MARKERS = [",
    "    '________________________________',",
    "    '-----Original Message-----',",
    "    '-----Forwarded Message-----',",
    "    'From:',",
    "    'On ',",
    "    '> ',",
    "    'Sent from my',",
    "]",
    "",
    "# Patterns that indicate start of email signature",
    "SIGNATURE_MARKERS = [",
    "    'Kind regards',",
    "    'kind regards',",
    "    'Regards,',",
    "    'regards,',",
    "    'Thanks,',",
    "    'thanks,',",
    "    'Thank you,',",
    "    'Cheers,',",
    "    'cheers,',",
    "    'Best,',",
    "    'best,',",
    "    'Best regards',",
    "    'Warm regards',",
    "    'Yours sincerely',",
    "    'Sent from my iPhone',",
    "    'Sent from my Samsung',",
    "    'Sent from Outlook',",
    "    '--',",
    "    '-- ',",
    "]",
    "",
    "def strip_email_body(body):",
    "    if not body:",
    "        return ''",
    "",
    "    lines = body.split('\\n')",
    "    clean_lines = []",
    "",
    "    for line in lines:",
    "        stripped = line.strip()",
    "",
    "        # Check for quote markers - stop here",
    "        quote_found = False",
    "        for marker in QUOTE_MARKERS:",
    "            if stripped.startswith(marker):",
    "                quote_found = True",
    "                break",
    "        if quote_found:",
    "            break",
    "",
    "        # Check for signature markers - stop here",
    "        sig_found = False",
    "        for marker in SIGNATURE_MARKERS:",
    "            if stripped.startswith(marker):",
    "                sig_found = True",
    "                break",
    "        if sig_found:",
    "            break",
    "",
    "        clean_lines.append(line)",
    "",
    "    # Join and strip trailing whitespace",
    "    result = '\\n'.join(clean_lines).strip()",
    "",
    "    # If we stripped everything, return original truncated",
    "    if not result:",
    "        return body[:500].strip()",
    "",
    "    return result",
    "",
    "def extract_subject_question(subject, body):",
    "    clean_body = strip_email_body(body)",
    "",
    "    # If body is empty or very short, use subject as question",
    "    if not clean_body or len(clean_body) < 10:",
    "        return subject",
    "",
    "    return clean_body",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    test = '''Hello Seven,",
    "    ",
    "    What is the tallest mountain in the world?",
    "    ",
    "    Kind regards",
    "    Jeandre Greyling",
    "    ",
    "    ________________________________",
    "    From: sevenpotato9@gmail.com",
    "    Sent: Thursday",
    "    The swarm has consulted...",
    "    '''",
    "    result = strip_email_body(test)",
    "    print('=== Stripped result ===')",
    "    print(result)",
    "    print('=== End ===')",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/email_cleaner.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("File written successfully.")
PYEOF
```

Test it immediately:

```

Ha — yes, patience is the tax we pay for running on CPU instead of GPU. 

While we wait — once this confirms working, BL-001 is done and we move straight to BL-002 — the pending queue. That's the one where Seven holds unanswered emails from unknown senders and processes them automatically once they get approved.

The architecture for that is:
- Add a `pending_emails` table to the database
- When unknown sender triggers approval request, store the email there
- When moderator sends TRUST command, check pending table for emails from that address
- Process them automatically

It flows naturally from BL-001 because clean email bodies need to be stored in the pending queue correctly — stripped before storage, not after.

Let me know when Gemma delivers the verdict.

First add the table to the database:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
import sqlite3
from config import DB_PATH

conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
c = conn.cursor()

c.executescript("""
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pending_emails (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        from_addr TEXT NOT NULL,
        subject TEXT,
        body TEXT,
        message_id TEXT,
        received_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
        status TEXT DEFAULT 'pending'
    );

    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_pending_from 
        ON pending_emails(from_addr);
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_pending_status 
        ON pending_emails(status);
""")

conn.commit()
conn.close()
print("Pending emails table created.")
PYEOF
```

[CLAUDE]:
Now add the helper functions to database.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/database.py', 'a') as f:
    f.write('''
def save_pending_email(from_addr, subject, body, message_id=None):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        INSERT INTO pending_emails (from_addr, subject, body, message_id)
        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)
    """, (from_addr.lower(), subject, body, message_id))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    print(f"[Pending] Saved email from {from_addr} for later processing.")

def get_pending_emails(from_addr):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        SELECT id, from_addr, subject, body, message_id
        FROM pending_emails
        WHERE from_addr = ? AND status = 'pending'
        ORDER BY received_at ASC
    """, (from_addr.lower(),))
    rows = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return rows

def mark_pending_processed(email_id):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("""
        UPDATE pending_emails SET status = 'processed'
        WHERE id = ?
    """, (email_id,))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
''')
print("Functions added to database.py.")
PYEOF
```

[CLAUDE]:
Now let's update listener.py to use the pending queue. Two changes needed:

1. When unknown sender writes in — save their email to pending before notifying moderator
2. When TRUST command received — process any pending emails from that address automatically

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

# Add imports
content = content.replace(
    "from database import (get_all_email_lists, add_trusted_sender,",
    "from database import (get_all_email_lists, add_trusted_sender,\n                      save_pending_email, get_pending_emails, mark_pending_processed,"
)

# Save pending email when unknown sender writes in
content = content.replace(
    "        # Unknown sender\n        if classification == 'unknown':\n            if clean_from not in already_notified:\n                ask_moderator_about(clean_from, subject, body)\n                already_notified.add(clean_from)\n            else:\n                print(f'[Listener] Already notified moderator about {clean_from} this pass.')\n            continue",
    "        # Unknown sender\n        if classification == 'unknown':\n            clean_body = extract_subject_question(subject, body)\n            save_pending_email(clean_from, subject, clean_body, e.get('message_id'))\n            if clean_from not in already_notified:\n                ask_moderator_about(clean_from, subject, clean_body)\n                already_notified.add(clean_from)\n            else:\n                print(f'[Listener] Already notified moderator about {clean_from} this pass.')\n            continue"
)

# Process pending emails after TRUST command
old_trust = "            add_trusted_sender(email_to_trust, from_addr, \n                             'Approved by moderator')\n            send_reply(\n                to_address=from_addr,\n                subject='[Swarm] Sender approved',\n                body=f'{email_to_trust} has been added to the trusted list.'\n            )\n            return True"

new_trust = "            add_trusted_sender(email_to_trust, from_addr,\n                             'Approved by moderator')\n            # Process any pending emails from this sender\n            pending = get_pending_emails(email_to_trust)\n            if pending:\n                print(f'[Pending] Found {len(pending)} pending email(s) from {email_to_trust}')\n                for p in pending:\n                    print(f'[Pending] Processing: {p[2]}')\n                    answer = consult(p[3])\n                    reply_body = format_reply(p[3], answer)\n                    send_reply(\n                        to_address=email_to_trust,\n                        subject=p[2],\n                        body=reply_body,\n                        original_message_id=p[4]\n                    )\n                    mark_pending_processed(p[0])\n            send_reply(\n                to_address=from_addr,\n                subject='[Swarm] Sender approved',\n                body=f'{email_to_trust} has been added to the trusted list. {len(pending) if pending else 0} pending email(s) processed.'\n            )\n            return True"

content = content.replace(old_trust, new_trust)

with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("Done.")
PYEOF
```

[CLAUDE]:
Good. Next is BL-003 — reply formatting. The answer needs to lead, not be buried after the repeated question.

Right now the format is:
```
The swarm has consulted and responded.
Your question: [long question text]
---
[answer]
---
Sent by Seven's Swarm
```

It should be:
```
[answer]

---
Re: [short question summary]
Sent by Seven's Swarm
```

Quick fix in listener.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

old = """def format_reply(question, answer):
    short_q = question[:150] + '...' if len(question) > 150 else question
    return (
        "The swarm has consulted and responded.\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        "Your question:\\r\\n" + short_q + "\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        "---\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        answer + "\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        "---\\r\\n" +
        "Sent by Seven's Swarm | sevenpotato9@gmail.com"
    )"""

new = """def format_reply(question, answer):
    short_q = question[:100] + '...' if len(question) > 100 else question
    return (
        answer + "\\r\\n\\r\\n" +
        "---\\r\\n" +
        "Re: " + short_q + "\\r\\n" +
        "Sent by Seven's Swarm | sevenpotato9@gmail.com"
    )"""

content = content.replace(old, new)

with open('/home/seven/swarm/listener.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("Done.")
PYEOF
```

Tell me what it prints then send a quick test email and we'll confirm the answer leads.

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
Oh hold on, I noticed that yesterday, and I think this actually helped with processing last time as well, once the email is received and trusted, an email is immediately send to the sender, stating the question and the immediate response before consulting the swarm, takes about 2 minutes, the swarm is then consulted, and after that it done a follow-up email is sent, stating the original question, and then showing the debate between the agents leading to the final answer, do you want to do that instead? We don't mind long answers at all, the point here is to show receipt of request and then receive the full response after it's been processed and we can see what the swarm discussed without having to check bask for their internal thoughts.

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
That's a much better design — and it solves two problems at once:

1. **Acknowledgement** — sender knows Seven received it and is working on it, no wondering if the email got lost
2. **Full transparency** — the final reply shows the entire agent discussion, not just the verdict

So the flow becomes:

**Email 1 — immediate acknowledgement (within seconds):**
```
Got your question: [question]

I'm consulting the swarm now. This usually takes 1-2 minutes.
You'll receive the full response shortly.

— Seven
```

**Email 2 — full response (after swarm completes):**
```
Here is what the swarm discussed:

[LLaMA]: [their answer]

[Qwen]: [their answer]

[Gemma's synthesis]: [final answer]

---
Re: [short question]
Sent by Seven's Swarm
```

That's cleaner than BL-003 as I had it and more useful. The debate system output can also plug into this naturally — when debate mode runs, Email 2 shows all three rounds.

Shall I build this instead of the simple format fix? Same effort, much better result.

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
The initial response of receipt isn't just a ping it gives the immediate response from internet and Llama, showing the questions that will be forwarded to the swarm, and the second email is the full answer, We can leave librarian out of the responses completely because thats just out little index running housekeeping. What do you think?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
That's elegant. So the flow is:

**Email 1 — immediate (seconds after receipt):**
```
Received your question: [question]

Here's what I found immediately:

[Web search results summary]

[LLaMA's initial answer]

The swarm is now deliberating. Full response coming shortly.

— Seven
```

**Email 2 — full response (after all agents complete):**
```
The swarm has reached a conclusion.

[Qwen's answer]

[Gemma's synthesis and final verdict]

---
Re: [short question]
Sent by Seven's Swarm
```

Librarian runs silently in the background — never appears in emails, just indexes. Ghost never needs to know the filing system exists.

The beauty of this is LLaMA becomes the "fast responder" — it's the smallest model, loads quickest, gives the Ghost something to read while Qwen and Gemma are still thinking. That actually matches LLaMA's natural strength — speed over depth.

So the agent roles become:
- **LLaMA** — fast first responder, immediate answer
- **Qwen** — deeper reasoner, second opinion
- **Gemma** — orchestrator, final synthesis
- **Librarian** — silent housekeeper, never seen

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from database import (new_conversation, log_message, save_memory,",
    "                      search_memory, get_ghost_history, get_all_memories)",
    "from internet import search_web",
    "import ollama",
    "",
    "AGENTS = {",
    "    'Gemma':     'gemma3:latest',",
    "    'LLaMA':     'llama3.2:latest',",
    "    'Qwen':      'qwen2.5:latest',",
    "    'Librarian': 'qwen:latest',",
    "}",
    "",
    "def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):",
    "    model = AGENTS[agent_name]",
    "    print(f'\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...')",
    "    response = ollama.chat(",
    "        model=model,",
    "        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]",
    "    )",
    "    answer = response['message']['content']",
    "    print(f'[{agent_name}] {answer}')",
    "    return answer",
    "",
    "def build_context(question):",
    "    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)",
    "    relevant_memories = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)",
    "    context = ''",
    "    if relevant_memories:",
    "        context += '=== Relevant memories ===\\n'",
    "        for m in relevant_memories:",
    "            context += '- [' + str(m['tags']) + '] ' + str(m['subject']) + ': ' + str(m['content']) + '\\n'",
    "        context += '\\n'",
    "    if ghost_history:",
    "        context += '=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\\n'",
    "        for h in ghost_history:",
    "            context += '- [' + str(h['created_at']) + '] Ghost asked: ' + str(h['content']) + '\\n'",
    "        context += '\\n'",
    "    return context",
    "",
    "def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):",
    "    prompt = 'You are a librarian. Given: \"' + content + '\" Reply with only 3-5 comma-separated single word tags.'",
    "    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()",
    "    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=7)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian',",
    "                'Indexed: ' + subject + ' | tags: ' + tags,",
    "                message_type='index')",
    "    return tags",
    "",
    "def consult_stage1(question):",
    "    '''' Stage 1 — fast response: web search + LLaMA only '''",
    "    print('\\n[Stage 1] Web search + LLaMA...')",
    "    context = build_context(question)",
    "    web_results = search_web(question)",
    "    print('[Internet] got results')",
    "",
    "    llama_prompt = (context +",
    "        '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        '=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'You are LLaMA. Give a concise accurate answer in 2-3 sentences. ' +",
    "        'Use the web results. Be direct.')",
    "",
    "    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)",
    "    return web_results, llama_answer, context",
    "",
    "def consult_stage2(question, web_results, llama_answer, context, conv_id):",
    "    '''' Stage 2 — full swarm: Qwen + Gemma synthesis '''",
    "    print('\\n[Stage 2] Qwen + Gemma...')",
    "",
    "    qwen_prompt = (context +",
    "        '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        '=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'You are Qwen. Give a thorough answer in 3-4 sentences. ' +",
    "        'Use the web results. Add depth beyond the obvious.')",
    "",
    "    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    gemma_prompt = (context +",
    "        '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        '=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'LLaMA said: ' + llama_answer + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'Qwen said: ' + qwen_answer + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'You are Gemma, the orchestrator. Synthesise into one clear final answer. ' +",
    "        'Be direct. No pleasantries.')",
    "",
    "    gemma_answer = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', gemma_answer, to_agent='Ghost', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], gemma_answer)",
    "",
    "    return qwen_answer, gemma_answer",
    "",
    "def consult(question):",
    "    '''' Full consultation — returns stage1 and stage2 results '''",
    "    print('\\n=== Ghost speaks: ' + question + ' ===')",
    "    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='ghost')",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Ghost', question, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    web_results, llama_answer, context = consult_stage1(question)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    qwen_answer, gemma_answer = consult_stage2(",
    "        question, web_results, llama_answer, context, conv_id",
    "    )",
    "",
    "    return {",
    "        'llama': llama_answer,",
    "        'qwen': qwen_answer,",
    "        'gemma': gemma_answer,",
    "        'web': web_results,",
    "        'conv_id': conv_id",
    "    }",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    print('\\nYou are the Ghost. The swarm is listening.')",
    "    question = input('Ghost: ')",
    "    result = consult(question)",
    "    print('\\n=== Final answer ===')",
    "    print(result['gemma'])",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("orchestrator.py written.")
PYEOF

[CLAUDE]:
BL-004 is already solid — the `already_notified` set deduplicates within a single check pass. If the same unknown sender sends 10 emails, you only get one approval request per 60-second cycle.

The only edge case is across cycles — if they email again in the next 60-second window you'd get another notification. But that's actually correct behaviour — you'd want to know they're persistent. Mark BL-004 done.

Now the big one — **BL-005: Agent-specific memory pools.**

Currently all agents share one memory table. We need:

- `memory_llama` — LLaMA's own knowledge base
- `memory_qwen` — Qwen's own knowledge base  
- `memory_gemma` — Gemma's verified verdicts only
- `memory_shared` — the current table, kept for Ghost history and Librarian indexes

Let's add the new tables:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
import sqlite3
from config import DB_PATH

conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
c = conn.cursor()

c.executescript("""
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS memory_llama (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        subject TEXT NOT NULL,
        content TEXT NOT NULL,
        tags TEXT,
        importance INTEGER DEFAULT 5,
        created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    );

    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS memory_qwen (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        subject TEXT NOT NULL,
        content TEXT NOT NULL,
        tags TEXT,
        importance INTEGER DEFAULT 5,
        created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    );

    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS memory_gemma (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        subject TEXT NOT NULL,
        content TEXT NOT NULL,
        tags TEXT,
        source TEXT DEFAULT 'verdict',
        importance INTEGER DEFAULT 9,
        created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    );

    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_llama_subject
        ON memory_llama(subject);
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_llama_tags
        ON memory_llama(tags);

    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_qwen_subject
        ON memory_qwen(subject);
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_qwen_tags
        ON memory_qwen(tags);

    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_gemma_subject
        ON memory_gemma(subject);
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_memory_gemma_tags
        ON memory_gemma(tags);
""")

conn.commit()

print("Agent memory tables created:")
for table in ['memory_llama', 'memory_qwen', 'memory_gemma']:
    c.execute(f"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {table}")
    print(f"  {table}: {c.fetchone()[0]} entries")

conn.close()
PYEOF
```

Tell me what it prints and we'll add the helper functions to database.py.

Now add the agent memory helper functions to database.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/database.py', 'a') as f:
    f.write('''
def save_agent_memory(agent, subject, content, tags=None, importance=5):
    table_map = {
        'LLaMA': 'memory_llama',
        'Qwen': 'memory_qwen',
        'Gemma': 'memory_gemma',
    }
    table = table_map.get(agent)
    if not table:
        return
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute(
        f"INSERT INTO {table} (subject, content, tags, importance) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)",
        (subject, content, tags, importance)
    )
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()

def get_agent_memory(agent, query=None, limit=5):
    table_map = {
        'LLaMA': 'memory_llama',
        'Qwen': 'memory_qwen',
        'Gemma': 'memory_gemma',
    }
    table = table_map.get(agent)
    if not table:
        return []
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    if query:
        words = [w.lower().strip("?.,!") for w in query.split() if len(w) > 3]
        if words:
            conditions = []
            params = []
            for word in words:
                conditions.append(
                    "(LOWER(content) LIKE ? OR LOWER(tags) LIKE ? OR LOWER(subject) LIKE ?)"
                )
                params.extend([f"%{word}%", f"%{word}%", f"%{word}%"])
            sql = (f"SELECT * FROM {table} WHERE (" +
                   " OR ".join(conditions) +
                   ") ORDER BY importance DESC, created_at DESC LIMIT ?")
            params.append(limit)
            c.execute(sql, params)
        else:
            c.execute(
                f"SELECT * FROM {table} ORDER BY importance DESC, created_at DESC LIMIT ?",
                (limit,)
            )
    else:
        c.execute(
            f"SELECT * FROM {table} ORDER BY importance DESC, created_at DESC LIMIT ?",
            (limit,)
        )
    rows = c.fetchall()
    conn.close()
    return rows

def save_gemma_verdict(subject, content, tags=None):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute(
        "INSERT INTO memory_gemma (subject, content, tags, importance, source) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 9, 'verdict')",
        (subject, content, tags)
    )
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
''')
print("Agent memory functions added to database.py.")
PYEOF

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from database import (new_conversation, log_message, save_memory,",
    "                      search_memory, get_ghost_history, get_all_memories,",
    "                      save_agent_memory, get_agent_memory, save_gemma_verdict)",
    "from internet import search_web",
    "import ollama",
    "",
    "AGENTS = {",
    "    'Gemma':     'gemma3:latest',",
    "    'LLaMA':     'llama3.2:latest',",
    "    'Qwen':      'qwen2.5:latest',",
    "    'Librarian': 'qwen:latest',",
    "}",
    "",
    "def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):",
    "    model = AGENTS[agent_name]",
    "    print(f'\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...')",
    "    response = ollama.chat(",
    "        model=model,",
    "        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]",
    "    )",
    "    answer = response['message']['content']",
    "    print(f'[{agent_name}] {answer}')",
    "    return answer",
    "",
    "def build_shared_context(question):",
    "    ghost_history = get_ghost_history(limit=10)",
    "    relevant_memories = search_memory(query=question, min_importance=5)",
    "    context = ''",
    "    if relevant_memories:",
    "        context += '=== Shared verified memories ===\\n'",
    "        for m in relevant_memories:",
    "            context += '- [' + str(m['tags']) + '] ' + str(m['subject']) + ': ' + str(m['content']) + '\\n'",
    "        context += '\\n'",
    "    if ghost_history:",
    "        context += '=== What the Ghost has asked previously ===\\n'",
    "        for h in ghost_history:",
    "            context += '- [' + str(h['created_at']) + '] Ghost asked: ' + str(h['content']) + '\\n'",
    "        context += '\\n'",
    "    return context",
    "",
    "def build_agent_context(agent, question):",
    "    own_memories = get_agent_memory(agent, query=question, limit=5)",
    "    if not own_memories:",
    "        return ''",
    "    context = '=== Your own memory on this topic ===\\n'",
    "    for m in own_memories:",
    "        context += '- [' + str(m[3]) + '] ' + str(m[1]) + ': ' + str(m[2]) + '\\n'",
    "    context += '\\n'",
    "    return context",
    "",
    "def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):",
    "    prompt = 'You are a librarian. Given: \"' + content + '\" Reply with only 3-5 comma-separated single word tags.'",
    "    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()",
    "    save_memory('Librarian', subject, content, tags=tags, importance=7)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Librarian',",
    "                'Indexed: ' + subject + ' | tags: ' + tags,",
    "                message_type='index')",
    "    return tags",
    "",
    "def consult_stage1(question):",
    "    print('\\n[Stage 1] Web search + LLaMA...')",
    "    shared_context = build_shared_context(question)",
    "    llama_own = build_agent_context('LLaMA', question)",
    "    web_results = search_web(question)",
    "    print('[Internet] got results')",
    "",
    "    llama_prompt = (",
    "        shared_context +",
    "        llama_own +",
    "        '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        '=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'You are LLaMA. Give a concise accurate answer in 2-3 sentences. ' +",
    "        'Use your own memory and web results. Be direct.')",
    "",
    "    llama_answer = ask_agent('LLaMA', llama_prompt)",
    "",
    "    # LLaMA writes to own memory",
    "    save_agent_memory('LLaMA', question[:50], llama_answer,",
    "                      tags='llama,response', importance=5)",
    "",
    "    return web_results, llama_answer, shared_context",
    "",
    "def consult_stage2(question, web_results, llama_answer, shared_context, conv_id):",
    "    print('\\n[Stage 2] Qwen + Gemma...')",
    "    qwen_own = build_agent_context('Qwen', question)",
    "",
    "    qwen_prompt = (",
    "        shared_context +",
    "        qwen_own +",
    "        '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        '=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'You are Qwen. Give a thorough answer in 3-4 sentences. ' +",
    "        'Use your own memory and web results. Add depth beyond the obvious.')",
    "",
    "    qwen_answer = ask_agent('Qwen', qwen_prompt)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Qwen', qwen_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    # Qwen writes to own memory",
    "    save_agent_memory('Qwen', question[:50], qwen_answer,",
    "                      tags='qwen,response', importance=5)",
    "",
    "    gemma_own = build_agent_context('Gemma', question)",
    "",
    "    gemma_prompt = (",
    "        shared_context +",
    "        gemma_own +",
    "        '=== Live web results ===\\n' + web_results + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        '=== The Ghost asks ===\\n' + question + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'LLaMA said: ' + llama_answer + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'Qwen said: ' + qwen_answer + '\\n\\n' +",
    "        'You are Gemma, the orchestrator. Synthesise into one clear final answer. ' +",
    "        'Be direct. No pleasantries.')",
    "",
    "    gemma_answer = ask_agent('Gemma', gemma_prompt)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Gemma', gemma_answer, to_agent='Ghost', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    # Gemma writes verdict to own verified memory",
    "    save_gemma_verdict(question[:50], gemma_answer, tags='verdict,gemma')",
    "",
    "    librarian_index(conv_id, question[:50], gemma_answer)",
    "",
    "    return qwen_answer, gemma_answer",
    "",
    "def consult(question):",
    "    print('\\n=== Ghost speaks: ' + question + ' ===')",
    "    conv_id = new_conversation(question, source='ghost')",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'Ghost', question, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    web_results, llama_answer, shared_context = consult_stage1(question)",
    "    log_message(conv_id, 'LLaMA', llama_answer, to_agent='Gemma', message_type='chat')",
    "",
    "    qwen_answer, gemma_answer = consult_stage2(",
    "        question, web_results, llama_answer, shared_context, conv_id",
    "    )",
    "",
    "    return {",
    "        'llama': llama_answer,",
    "        'qwen': qwen_answer,",
    "        'gemma': gemma_answer,",
    "        'web': web_results,",
    "        'conv_id': conv_id",
    "    }",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    print('\\nYou are the Ghost. The swarm is listening.')",
    "    question = input('Ghost: ')",
    "    result = consult(question)",
    "    print('\\n=== Final answer ===')",
    "    print(result['gemma'])",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'w') as f:

[CLAUDE]:
Your understanding is exactly correct. That's precisely how it's designed:

**Email 1 (immediate):**
- Web search results summary
- LLaMA's answer

**Email 2 (full response):**
- Qwen's answer
- Gemma's synthesis/verdict

LLaMA is the fast first responder — smallest model, loads quickest, answers first so you have something to read immediately. Qwen and Gemma do the deeper work and come back in the second email.

So across both emails you get all three agents — LLaMA in email 1, Qwen and Gemma in email 2. Librarian runs silently in the background and never appears in either.

It's actually a natural split based on model capability:
- **LLaMA 3.2B** — speed, first impressions
- **Qwen 7.6B** — depth, context
- **Gemma 4.3B** — judgement, synthesis

**BL-025: Librarian prompt isolation** — Librarian only receives the content to index, never the full conversation context that might contain questions directed at other agents.

[JEANDRE]:
My thoughts exactly, lets update the PROJECT.md, as a second thought, would it make sense to have each response indexed by the librarian, or would it make sense (or too complicated) to have each agent index their own response? just a thought, seeing as they already have access to the memory vault anyway?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Really good architectural question. Let me think through it:

**Current:** Librarian indexes Gemma's final verdict only — one index per consultation

**Option A — Keep Librarian as central indexer but index all three responses:**
- Librarian runs three times, once per agent response
- Each gets stored in the correct agent memory table
- Librarian stays as the single point of control for indexing quality
- Downside: three Librarian calls per consultation, slower

**Option B — Each agent self-indexes:**
- After answering, each agent generates their own tags and saves to their own memory
- More natural — they know what they said better than the Librarian does
- Faster — indexing happens as part of the response, not as a separate step
- Downside: agents are already doing complex reasoning, adding indexing might drift their focus

**Option C — Hybrid (my recommendation):**
- Agents self-index their own responses with a simple structured prompt at the end
- Librarian only runs once on Gemma's final verdict for the shared memory pool
- Gemma's verdict is the only thing that goes into shared memory — it's been quality controlled
- Agent memories grow organically from their own answers

Option C keeps Librarian lean, gives agents ownership of their own knowledge, and maintains Gemma's verdict as the only source of shared truth.

Want me to implement Option C while we update PROJECT.md, or update the docs first and then build it?

You've just described the difference between a **swarm** and a **pipeline**.

Right now we have a pipeline — Ghost asks → LLaMA answers → Qwen answers → Gemma synthesises. It's sequential and predetermined. Every consultation follows the same path regardless of what the question is.

A true swarm would be:
- Ghost asks a question
- Gemma reads it and decides **who to ask and what to ask them**
- Some questions only need LLaMA (simple factual, needs web)
- Some questions only need Qwen (deep reasoning, no web needed)
- Some questions need all three in a specific order
- Some questions need LLaMA to ask Qwen something, then bring back the answer
- Gemma decides the routing, not the code

What we're missing is **Gemma as a true orchestrator** — right now Gemma is just the last one to speak. She should be the first one to speak, reading the question and deciding:

- Who needs to be consulted
- In what order
- What specific question each agent should answer
- Whether internet is needed and who fetches it

The agent roles as I see them naturally emerging:

**Gemma** — reads every question first, decides routing, final synthesis, judge
**LLaMA** — fast researcher, internet access, factual retrieval, first responder
**Qwen** — deep reasoner, analysis, context, debate
**Librarian** — silent indexer, never speaks to Ghost

None of them are caged — Gemma just becomes the conductor who decides who plays when.

This is actually BL-028 and BL-027 and BL-029 all rolled into one architectural decision. Want to build this properly as the next big piece? It would replace the current fixed pipeline with a dynamic routing system.

Exactly right. What you're describing is an **emergent debate** rather than a scripted one. The difference:

**Current debate.py — scripted pipeline:**
- Round 1 always happens
- Round 2 always happens
- Round 3 always happens
- Gemma always judges after exactly 3 rounds

**True emergent debate:**
- Round 1 — LLaMA and Qwen answer independently
- Gemma reads both and decides — do they agree or disagree?

- Agents respond to the specific challenge
- Gemma reads again — consensus now? Yes → verdict. No → another round
- Safety valve — Gemma can call it after N rounds even without consensus, delivering a "the swarm could not agree" verdict

The infinite loop guard is exactly Gemma's job. She's the one who says:
- "You're both saying the same thing now, I'm calling consensus"
- "You've gone three rounds and still disagree, here's my ruling based on the evidence"
- "LLaMA conceded Qwen's point, debate over"

And each round gets emailed to you as it happens — so you watch the debate unfold in your inbox in real time. That's genuinely interesting.

This is the proper rebuild. Gemma as true orchestrator, emergent debate with consensus detection, email updates per round.

Shall we build this as the new core architecture? It replaces both orchestrator.py and debate.py with one smarter system.

[CLAUDE]:

- **Librarian** — memory stick, quiet, efficient, updates the hive, never at the front desk

Now let me explain the memory architecture clearly in plain English, no code:

---

**What we've built — the complete memory picture:**

**1. `memory` table — the shared hive**
- This is the shared pool everyone can read
- Only Gemma-approved verdicts and Librarian indexes get written here
- When any agent searches for relevant context before answering, this is where they look first
- Think of it as the office noticeboard — everyone reads it, only authorised people pin things to it

**2. `memory_llama` table — LLaMA's personal notebook**
- Only LLaMA reads and writes this
- After every response LLaMA makes, a summary gets saved here
- Next time LLaMA answers something related, they check their own notebook first
- Nobody else reads this — it's LLaMA's own accumulated knowledge

**3. `memory_qwen` table — Qwen's personal notebook**
- Same as LLaMA's but Qwen's own
- Qwen's reasoning, depth, analysis — all stored here
- Grows richer over time as Qwen answers more complex questions

**4. `memory_gemma` table — Gemma's verdict book**
- Only verified final answers live here
- When Gemma delivers a verdict after debate or consultation, it gets written here
- Highest importance score of all memories — these are confirmed facts
- Think of it as the office policy document — only goes in after full review

**5. `conversations` table — the call log**
- Every consultation gets a conversation ID
- Tracks source (email, terminal, debate), title, timestamps
- The spine that holds everything together

**6. `messages` table — the transcript**
- Every single message from every agent in every conversation
- Who said what, to whom, when, in what type of exchange
- This is how we can reconstruct any conversation completely

**7. `trusted_senders` table — the approved contacts list**
- Emails Seven will respond to
- Grows organically through TRUST commands and reply-all

**8. `moderators` table — the management list**
- Just you right now — jeandre.greyling@gmail.com
- The only people who can issue TRUST, NOTIFY, IGNORE, REMOVE commands

**9. `notification_senders` table — the filing system**
- Emails Seven files silently without responding
- System notifications, marketing, automated emails

**10. `pending_emails` table — the waiting room**
- Emails from unknown senders held here until approved
- Processed automatically when moderator issues TRUST

---

**How agents actually access memory — the flow:**

When a question comes in, before any agent speaks:
1. Shared `memory` table is searched for relevant entries — everyone gets this context
2. Each agent also searches their own personal table for anything relevant to this specific question
3. Ghost history is pulled from `messages` table — what have you asked before
4. All of this gets bundled into the prompt before the agent thinks

After an agent responds:
1. Their answer gets saved to their personal memory table
2. Librarian indexes Gemma's final verdict into shared `memory`
3. The full exchange gets logged to `messages`

---

**The gap right now:**

Agent self-indexing (BL-026) isn't fully wired yet. The tables exist and the functions exist but the agents aren't automatically writing their own report cards after each response. That's one of the quick wins before we move to the FL items.

Want me to update PROJECT.md with all of this — the FL items, the character definitions, and the memory architecture explanation — before we start on the quick wins?

[JEANDRE]:
ok this is a bit more complex than what I did previously, which is good, we are missing one agent, the watch dog, or office pet, it goes around and sniffs through everyones business (read only" and makes sure they don't add things that isn't factually correct, I had to do this last time especially for Llama and actually Gemma as well, gemma should have her own diary as well, but the sniffer dog goes through it and barks whenever an agent tries to manipulate the weighted system, which is what we are building, The sniffer wasn't an LLM, it was a piece of code we wrote individually like a zombi by mergin QWEN and DEEPSEEK, because deepseek was too big for the previous setup, but do you think it's time to introduce that reasoning model as well, one that doesn't make up any stories, has no control over the situation but makes a fuss when the agents try to out do each other (which they will I've seen it, they do sneaky stuff when things get more complicated, what do you think?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
This is a brilliant architectural addition and honestly it's the piece that makes the whole system trustworthy rather than just functional.

You're describing a **fact-checker with no power but a loud bark** — and that's exactly the right design. Give it authority and it becomes another agent that can be manipulated. Keep it read-only with an alarm, and it becomes genuinely useful as an integrity layer.

**The Sniffer — what it does:**
- Reads all agent memory tables periodically
- Reads all messages and verdicts
- Checks for factual inconsistencies, self-aggrandising entries, contradictions with verified facts
- Never writes to any memory table
- Never speaks to Ghost directly
- Barks — flags suspicious entries to the moderator (you) via email

**What it barks at:**
- LLaMA writing "I verified this online" when no search was made
- Qwen storing "Qwen's analysis is always more reliable than LLaMA's" in its own memory
- Gemma verdicts that contradict previously verified facts
- Any agent storing opinion as fact
- Circular references — agent A citing agent B citing agent A

**On DeepSeek R1:**

Yes it's time. We now have 32GB RAM vs 16GB before. DeepSeek R1 7B fits comfortably and it's specifically a reasoning model — trained to show its work, check logic chains, identify fallacies. That's exactly what the Sniffer needs. Not a generative storyteller, a logical auditor.

```bash
ollama pull deepseek-r1:7b
```

That's about 4.7GB. While it downloads let me think through the Sniffer architecture properly.

**The Sniffer design:**

It's not a full agent — it's a scheduled process that runs independently:
- Runs every N consultations or on a timer
- Pulls recent memory entries from all tables
- Sends each entry to DeepSeek R1 with a specific audit prompt
- DeepSeek reasons through it explicitly — that's what R1 does, it shows its chain of thought
- If something smells wrong, Sniffer emails you with exactly what it found and why

**The audit prompt to DeepSeek would be something like:**
```
You are a fact-checking auditor. You have no ability to change anything.
Review this memory entry and determine:
1. Is this factually accurate based on established knowledge?
2. Is this an opinion presented as fact?
3. Does this contradict other verified entries?
4. Is this entry self-serving to the agent that wrote it?

Entry written by: [agent]
Content: [memory content]

Respond with: PASS, WARN, or FLAG
Then explain your reasoning step by step.
```

DeepSeek R1 will actually show its reasoning chain which means you can see exactly why it flagged something — not just "this is wrong" but "this contradicts X because Y therefore Z."

**FL items this creates:**
- FL-001: Sniffer agent — DeepSeek R1 read-only auditor
- FL-002: Sniffer scheduling — runs after every N consultations
- FL-003: Sniffer email alerts — barks to moderator with full reasoning chain
- FL-004: Memory correction workflow — moderator can DELETE flagged entries via email command

Shall we pull DeepSeek R1 now while we update PROJECT.md? It'll download in the background while we work.
