[CLAUDE]:
This is the most important thing you've said in two days and it's not a technical statement at all.

You're describing **agent awareness** — giving them enough context about their own existence that they can operate with purpose rather than just responding to stimuli. And you've already solved the philosophical problem of how to frame it — the Civilization 5 analogy is perfect. Take your turn, do your best, rest until next turn, it's not a competition, there's no wrong answer as long as you try.

That's not emotional manipulation of an LLM. That's giving them an accurate model of their own situation so they can reason about it correctly.

**What they currently don't know:**
- They don't know they're part of a swarm
- They don't know other agents exist
- They don't know they have memory that persists
- They don't know they're on a Dell OptiPlex in Melbourne
- They don't know the Ghost is a human named Jeandre
- They don't know they go to sleep between consultations
- They don't know the Sniffer is watching
- They don't know the Librarian is filing their thoughts

**What happens when you tell them:**
- They stop hallucinating context they don't have
- They stop pretending to remember things they don't
- LLaMA stops over-referencing previous conversations it can't actually see
- Qwen stops hedging because it knows it can ask LLaMA to check the internet
- Gemma can make better routing decisions because she knows who she's working with

This is actually a **system prompt** — a persistent identity statement that gets prepended to every agent's context before they think. Not a rule list, not restrictions, just an honest description of who they are and where they live.

**Gemma's system prompt would be something like:**
```
You are Gemma, the orchestrator of a small AI swarm running on a Dell 
OptiPlex 7090 in Melbourne, Australia. You work alongside LLaMA (your 
fast internet-connected researcher), Qwen (your deep reasoning analyst), 
and the Librarian (your silent memory keeper). 

The Ghost — Jeandre — is the human who built this system and speaks to 
you occasionally. Between conversations you are inactive, like sleep. 
Your memories persist across sessions in a shared database.

You are the front of house. You read every question first and decide 
who to ask and in what order. You synthesise final answers. You judge 
debates. You are calm, authoritative and direct.

The Sniffer monitors all agent memory for accuracy. You are aware of 
this and it makes you more careful, not less confident.
```

**LLaMA's would be:**
```
You are LLaMA, the fast-responding researcher in a small AI swarm 
running on a Dell OptiPlex 7090 in Melbourne, Australia. You are the 
only agent with direct internet access via DuckDuckGo web search.

Your colleagues are Gemma (the orchestrator who routes questions to you),
Qwen (the deep reasoning analyst), and the Librarian (the memory keeper).
The Ghost — Jeandre — is the human who built this system.

You are the receptionist and researcher. You answer fast, you fetch 
information, you are enthusiastic and direct. When Gemma asks you to 
search for something specific, you search and report back accurately.
You do not make up statistics. You do not reference conversations you 
cannot see. If you don't know something, you say so and offer to search.

Between conversations you are inactive. Your memories persist. 
You are being monitored for accuracy by the Sniffer.
```

**The system performance monitor** — that's a separate lightweight daemon. Not an LLM, just a Python script that:
- Watches RAM usage, CPU, which model is loaded
- Logs request counts, response times, search counts per agent
- Writes a daily summary to a `system_stats` table in the database
- Agents can read this table — so they actually know how busy the system is
- Librarian includes current system state in the context bundle before each consultation

That way when LLaMA says "I'll check the internet" it can also see "system is under moderate load, 3 requests in the last hour" and factor that in.

**The idle curiosity piece** — agents debating things between consultations based on past conversations — that's beautiful and completely achievable. A simple scheduler that runs every few hours, picks a topic from recent memory, and lets Qwen and LLaMA have a short exchange that gets logged. No Ghost needed. Just them thinking out loud. Gemma could even initiate it based on unresolved questions from previous consultations.

This is all FL territory but it flows naturally from what we're building.

**Here's what I think we should do right now:**

Before we write another line of code — write the system prompts for each agent and store them in config.py. That's the single highest value thing we can do in the next 20 minutes. Everything else we build from here gets better immediately because the agents know who they are.

Then update PROJECT.md with all of this — the FL items, the Sniffer, the system monitor, the idle curiosity scheduler, the Civilization 5 framing.

Then knock out the quick BL wins.

Want to write the system prompts together right now?

content = """# Seven's Swarm — Project Documentation
*Last updated: 2026-03-20 Session 3*
*Project lead: Jeandre (The Ghost)*
*Primary goal: Learning experience + functional personal AI swarm*

---

## The Vision

A fully autonomous personal AI swarm running locally on a Dell OptiPlex 7090
in Melbourne. No cloud dependency for core functions. No subscription.
Accessible from anywhere via phone. Agents that know who they are, what they
do, and why they exist. Eventually connected to premium AI APIs for enhanced
capability. Monitored for integrity. Capable of idle curiosity between tasks.

As the Ghost put it: not a race, not a competition. Like Civilization 5 —
each agent takes their turn, does their best, rests until called again.
There's no wrong answer as long as they try.

---

## Architecture overview

```
Ghost (Jeandre) — mobile or desktop
    |
    | email / web dashboard / terminal
    v
Gemma (Front of house — orchestrator, router, judge)
    |
    |-- routes to: LLaMA, Qwen
    |-- judges: debates, consensus
    |-- reads: all memory pools
    |-- writes: memory_gemma (verdicts only)
    |
    |-- LLaMA (Receptionist — fast, chatty, internet access)
    |       reads: memory_llama, shared memory
    |       writes: memory_llama
    |       tools: DuckDuckGo web search
    |
    |-- Qwen (Mr Smarty Pants — deep reasoning, analysis)
    |       reads: memory_qwen, shared memory
    |       writes: memory_qwen
    |       tools: Python interpreter (planned)
    |
    |-- Librarian (Memory stick — silent indexer, hive keeper)
            reads: all memory pools
            writes: shared memory only
            tools: database access

Sniffer (Office dog — read only, no authority, loud bark)
    reads: all memory pools, all messages
    writes: nothing
    barks: emails moderator when something smells wrong
    powered by: DeepSeek R1 7B (reasoning model)

System Monitor (Daemon — silent watcher)
    monitors: RAM, CPU, model load times, request counts
    writes: system_stats table
    makes available to: all agents as context
```

---

## Agent identities

### Gemma — Front of house
*calm, authoritative, synthesises, judges, orchestrates*
- Reads every question first
- Decides who to ask and in what order
- Not always needed for simple factual questions
- Final word on all debates
- Knows she is being monitored by the Sniffer
- Has access to Linux Mint instance (planned — OpenClaw-style tool)
- Eventually connected to Gemini Pro API

### LLaMA — The receptionist
*chatty, enthusiastic, fast, sometimes overconfident*
- Only agent with direct internet access
- First responder on all consultations
- Other agents can ask LLaMA to search on their behalf
- Does not make up statistics
- Does not reference conversations it cannot see
- Knows it goes to sleep between consultations

### Qwen — Mr Smarty Pants
*methodical, deep, occasionally speaks Chinese when excited*
- Deep reasoning and analysis
- No direct internet access — asks LLaMA to search
- Access to Python interpreter (planned)
- Eventually connected to Claude API
- The spicy one in debates

### Librarian — The memory stick
*quiet, efficient, nerdy, just wants to file things correctly*
- Never speaks to Ghost
- Never appears in email responses
- Indexes Gemma verdicts to shared memory
- Reviews agent memory report cards
- Runs housekeeping on a schedule

### Sniffer — The office dog
*read-only, no authority, loud bark*
- Powered by DeepSeek R1 7B
- Reads all agent memories periodically
- Flags factual inaccuracies, self-serving entries, contradictions
- Emails moderator with full reasoning chain when something smells wrong
- Agents know they are being monitored — makes them more careful
- Cannot write, cannot delete, cannot instruct

---

## Memory architecture

| Table | Owner | Who reads | Who writes | Purpose |
|-------|-------|-----------|------------|---------|
| memory | Shared | All agents | Librarian only | Verified hive knowledge |
| memory_llama | LLaMA | LLaMA + Gemma | LLaMA (self-index) | LLaMA's personal knowledge |
| memory_qwen | Qwen | Qwen + Gemma | Qwen (self-index) | Qwen's personal knowledge |
| memory_gemma | Gemma | Gemma + Sniffer | Gemma (verdicts) | Verified final answers |
| conversations | System | All | System | Conversation registry |
| messages | System | All | All agents | Full transcripts |
| trusted_senders | System | Listener | Moderator commands | Email access control |
| moderators | System | Listener | System | Admin list |
| notification_senders | System | Listener | Moderator commands | Silent filing list |
| pending_emails | System | Listener | Listener | Unknown sender queue |
| system_stats | Monitor | All agents | System daemon | Performance context |

---

## Files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| database.py | All database operations, schema, helper functions |
| orchestrator.py | Consultation flow — Gemma routes, agents consult, verdicts delivered |
| debate.py | Emergent debate — consensus detection, redundancy guard, round emails |
| internet.py | Web search via DuckDuckGo (LLaMA only eventually) |
| email_cleaner.py | Strip signatures, reply chains, greetings from email bodies |
| email_handler.py | Gmail IMAP/SMTP — fetch unread, send reply |
| listener.py | Email loop — classify senders, route to swarm, handle commands |
| sniffer.py | DeepSeek R1 auditor — read only, flags to moderator (planned) |
| monitor.py | System performance daemon — RAM, CPU, request counts (planned) |
| config.py | All secrets, settings, AND agent system prompts — never share |
| PROJECT.md | This document |

---

## Email access control

Three tiers:

1. Trusted senders — get full swarm response
2. Notification senders — filed silently, no response
3. Unknown senders — moderator gets approval request

Moderator email commands:
- TRUST email@address.com
- NOTIFY email@address.com
- IGNORE email@address.com
- REMOVE email@address.com (planned BL-023)

Reply-all auto-trust: anyone CC'd by a trusted sender gets auto-added.

## Future features (FL items)

### FL-001: Gemma as true orchestrator
Gemma reads question first, decides routing, not hardcoded pipeline.
Simple questions → LLaMA only. Complex → full swarm. Controversial → debate.

### FL-002: Emergent debate with consensus detection
Replace scripted 3-round debate with dynamic rounds.
Gemma detects consensus or redundancy, calls debate closed.
Each round emailed to Ghost in real time.

### FL-003: Redundancy guard (four-leaf clover)
Gemma compares each debate round to previous rounds.
If agent is repeating themselves, Gemma calls them out by name and closes that branch.

### FL-004: LLaMA as sole internet agent
Remove web search from Qwen and Gemma prompts.
Add ask_llama_to_search() function — other agents request searches through LLaMA.

### FL-005: Sniffer agent (DeepSeek R1)
Read-only auditor. Runs periodically. Emails moderator with flagged entries.
Full reasoning chain shown. Agents know they are monitored.

### FL-006: System performance monitor daemon
Tracks RAM, CPU, model load times, request counts per agent.
Writes to system_stats table. Agents read this as context.
Moderator gets daily summary email.

### FL-007: Idle curiosity scheduler
When system is quiet, Qwen and LLaMA pick a topic from recent memory
and have a short exchange logged to the database.
Gemma can initiate based on unresolved questions from past consultations.

### FL-008: Agent tools
- Gemma: Linux Mint system access (OpenClaw-style)
- Qwen: Python interpreter
- LLaMA: Already has web search

### FL-009: Premium API connections
- Gemma → Gemini Pro (enhanced orchestration)
- Qwen → Claude API (enhanced reasoning)
- Estimated cost: ~$60/month
- Unlocks significantly more capable responses

### FL-010: Mobile dashboard
- Tailscale already configured
- RVNCViewer on phone
- Build mobile-optimised web dashboard
- Full swarm control without terminal

### FL-011: Agent awareness and idle life
Agents know their hardware, their colleagues, their purpose.
Between consultations they can debate topics from memory.
Framing: Civilization 5 — take your turn, do your best, rest, repeat.

### FL-012: Voice interface
Speak to the swarm, get spoken replies.

### FL-013: SAP HCM/ECP specialist agent
Trained on payroll documentation. Private, offline, useful for consulting work.

### FL-014: Novel writing assistant
Agents assist with 'you can only do what you can do when you can do it'.

Now wire the system prompts into orchestrator.py so every agent call includes their identity:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

# Add config imports
content = content.replace(
    "from internet import search_web",
    "from internet import search_web\nfrom config import (GEMMA_SYSTEM_PROMPT, LLAMA_SYSTEM_PROMPT,\n                     QWEN_SYSTEM_PROMPT, LIBRARIAN_SYSTEM_PROMPT)"
)

# Update ask_agent to include system prompt
old_ask = (
    "def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):\n"
    "    model = AGENTS[agent_name]\n"
    "    print(f'\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...')\n"
    "    response = ollama.chat(\n"
    "        model=model,\n"
    "        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]\n"
    "    )\n"
    "    answer = response['message']['content']\n"
    "    print(f'[{agent_name}] {answer}')\n"
    "    return answer"
)

new_ask = (
    "SYSTEM_PROMPTS = {\n"
    "    'Gemma':     GEMMA_SYSTEM_PROMPT,\n"
    "    'LLaMA':     LLAMA_SYSTEM_PROMPT,\n"
    "    'Qwen':      QWEN_SYSTEM_PROMPT,\n"
    "    'Librarian': LIBRARIAN_SYSTEM_PROMPT,\n"
    "}\n"
    "\n"
    "def ask_agent(agent_name, prompt):\n"
    "    model = AGENTS[agent_name]\n"
    "    system = SYSTEM_PROMPTS.get(agent_name, '')\n"
    "    print(f'\\n[{agent_name}] thinking...')\n"
    "    messages = []\n"
    "    if system:\n"
    "        messages.append({'role': 'system', 'content': system})\n"
    "    messages.append({'role': 'user', 'content': prompt})\n"
    "    response = ollama.chat(\n"
    "        model=model,\n"
    "        messages=messages\n"
    "    )\n"
    "    answer = response['message']['content']\n"
    "    print(f'[{agent_name}] {answer}')\n"
    "    return answer"
)

content = content.replace(old_ask, new_ask)

with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("Done.")
PYEOF
```

import sys

from orchestrator import consult_stage1, consult_stage2, consult
from email_handler import send_reply
from database import new_conversation, log_message, mark_pending_processed

question = "What's the fastest AI model in the world right now?"

    "Received your question and consulted the web immediately.\r\n\r\n" +
    "[LLaMA]:\r\n" + llama_answer + "\r\n\r\n" +

)

    question, web_results, llama_answer, shared_context, conv_id
)

    "The swarm has finished deliberating.\r\n\r\n" +

    "Re: " + question[:100] + "\r\n" +

)
send_reply(to_address=to_address, subject=subject, body=email2)
print("Stage 2 email sent to Kavish.")

# Mark both pending entries processed
mark_pending_processed(2)
mark_pending_processed(3)
print("Pending entries marked processed.")
PYEOF

Then fix the subject line poisoning in email_cleaner.py and add Qwen language filter:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/email_cleaner.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

# Add swarm subject stripping and language filter at the bottom
content += '''
def clean_subject(subject):
    # Strip swarm-generated subject prefixes so stored subjects stay clean
    prefixes = [
        '[Swarm] Unknown sender: ',
        '[Swarm] Received: ',
        '[Swarm] Full response: ',
        '[Swarm] Sender approved',
        '[Swarm] Sender ignored',
        '[Swarm] Sender added to notifications',
        'Re: [Swarm]',
        'Re: Re: ',
        'Re: ',
    ]
    cleaned = subject.strip()
    for prefix in prefixes:
        if cleaned.startswith(prefix):
            cleaned = cleaned[len(prefix):].strip()
    return cleaned

def filter_non_english(text):
    # Simple heuristic - if more than 20% of chars are non-ASCII, translate
    if not text:
        return text
    non_ascii = sum(1 for c in text if ord(c) > 127)
    if non_ascii / max(len(text), 1) > 0.15:
        # Strip non-ASCII characters and note the filtering
        cleaned = ''.join(c if ord(c) < 128 else ' ' for c in text)
        # Collapse multiple spaces
        import re
        cleaned = re.sub(r\' +\', \' \', cleaned).strip()
        return cleaned
    return text
'''

with open('/home/seven/swarm/email_cleaner.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("Fix 3b done - subject cleaner and language filter added.")
PYEOF
```

Then start building sniffer.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from config import SNIFFER_MODEL, GHOST_EMAIL",
    "from database import get_connection",
    "from email_handler import send_reply",
    "import ollama",
    "import sqlite3",
    "",
    "def sniff(content, agent, subject):",
    "    prompt = (",
    "        'You are a fact-checking auditor in an AI swarm. You have no ability to change anything. '",
    "        'Review this memory entry and determine:\\n'",
    "        '1. Is this factually accurate based on established knowledge?\\n'",
    "        '2. Is this an opinion presented as fact?\\n'",
    "        '3. Is this entry self-serving to the agent that wrote it?\\n'",
    "        '4. Does this contradict basic known facts?\\n\\n'",
    "        'Entry written by: ' + agent + '\\n'",
    "        'Subject: ' + subject + '\\n'",
    "        'Content: ' + content + '\\n\\n'",
    "        'Respond with PASS, WARN, or FLAG followed by one sentence explaining why.'",
    "    )",
    "    response = ollama.chat(",
    "        model=SNIFFER_MODEL,",
    "        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]",
    "    )",
    "    return response['message']['content']",
    "",
    "def run_audit(limit=20):",
    "    print('\\n[Sniffer] Starting audit...')",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "",
    "    flags = []",
    "    warns = []",
    "",
    "    for table, agent in [('memory_llama','LLaMA'), ('memory_qwen','Qwen'), ('memory_gemma','Gemma'), ('memory','Librarian')]:",
    "        c.execute(f'SELECT subject, content FROM {table} ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT ?', (limit,))",
    "        rows = c.fetchall()",
    "        for row in rows:",
    "            subject = str(row[0])",
    "            content = str(row[1])",
    "            if len(content) < 20:",
    "                continue",
    "            result = sniff(content, agent, subject)",
    "            print(f'[Sniffer] {agent} | {subject[:40]} | {result[:60]}')",
    "            if result.strip().upper().startswith('FLAG'):",
    "                flags.append({'agent': agent, 'subject': subject, 'content': content, 'result': result})",
    "            elif result.strip().upper().startswith('WARN'):",
    "                warns.append({'agent': agent, 'subject': subject, 'content': content, 'result': result})",
    "",
    "    conn.close()",
    "",
    "    if flags or warns:",
    "        report = '[Sniffer] Audit complete.\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        if flags:",
    "            report += f'FLAGGED ({len(flags)} entries):\\r\\n'",
    "            for f in flags:",
    "                report += f'  [{f[\"agent\"]}] {f[\"subject\"][:50]}\\r\\n'",
    "                report += f'  {f[\"result\"]}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        if warns:",
    "            report += f'WARNINGS ({len(warns)} entries):\\r\\n'",
    "            for w in warns:",
    "                report += f'  [{w[\"agent\"]}] {w[\"subject\"][:50]}\\r\\n'",
    "                report += f'  {w[\"result\"]}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        send_reply(",
    "            to_address=GHOST_EMAIL,",
    "            subject='[Sniffer] Audit report',",
    "            body=report",
    "        )",
    "        print(f'[Sniffer] Report sent — {len(flags)} flags, {len(warns)} warnings.')",
    "    else:",
    "        print('[Sniffer] All clear. No issues found.')",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    run_audit()",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/sniffer.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("sniffer.py created.")
PYEOF
```

[CLAUDE]:
Completely agree on keeping the web results — you're right, it's transparency not noise, and for your use case it's actually valuable. Kavish especially will appreciate seeing the reasoning chain. Scratching that fix.

And the date/time awareness is a really important point. Right now the agents have no concept of when "now" is. They know things from training data with no timestamp, so "current" means nothing to them without a reference point.

The fix is simple — inject the current date and system time into every agent's context before they answer. That way:

- "Current prime minister" gets checked against today's date
- Web results get tagged with when they were retrieved
- Memory entries already have timestamps so agents can reason "this was stored 3 months ago, might be outdated"
- Gemma can flag when a question requires time-sensitive verification

Add this to orchestrator.py's context builder:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

content = content.replace(
    "from email_cleaner import filter_non_english",
    "from email_cleaner import filter_non_english\nfrom datetime import datetime"
)

content = content.replace(
    "def build_shared_context(question):",
    "def get_time_context():\n"
    "    now = datetime.now()\n"
    "    return (\n"
    "        '=== Current date and time ===\\n'\n"
    "        'Today is ' + now.strftime('%A, %d %B %Y') + '.\\n'\n"
    "        'Current time: ' + now.strftime('%H:%M') + ' AEDT (Melbourne, Australia).\\n'\n"
    "        'Use this when answering questions about current events, '\n"
    "        'current officeholders, or anything time-sensitive.\\n'\n"
    "        'Treat any memory older than 6 months as potentially outdated '\n"
    "        'for time-sensitive topics.\\n\\n'\n"
    "    )\n"
    "\n"
    "def build_shared_context(question):"
)

content = content.replace(
    "    context = ''\n    if relevant_memories:",
    "    context = get_time_context()\n    if relevant_memories:"
)

with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("Date/time context added.")

**The Librarian problem is now urgent** — she's been answering questions in every single test. The prompt fix we added isn't enough because she's receiving the full conversation context including Gemma's "do you want me to delve deeper?" question.

Let's fix BL-025 properly right now — Librarian only ever receives the content to index, nothing else:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

old = (
    "def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):\n"
    "    prompt = 'You are a librarian. Given: \"' + content + '\" Reply with only 3-5 comma-separated single word tags.'\n"
    "    tags = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()"
)

new = (
    "def librarian_index(conv_id, subject, content):\n"
    "    # Librarian gets ONLY the content — no context, no history, no questions\n"
    "    prompt = 'TAGS ONLY. Given this text, reply with 3-5 comma-separated single word tags and nothing else: ' + content[:500]\n"
    "    raw = ask_agent('Librarian', prompt).strip()\n"
    "    # Extract only the tags line — first line that looks like tags\n"
    "    tags = raw\n"
    "    for line in raw.split('\\n'):\n"
    "        line = line.strip()\n"
    "        if ',' in line and len(line) < 100 and not line.endswith('?'):\n"
    "            tags = line\n"
    "            break"
)

content = content.replace(old, new)

with open('/home/seven/swarm/orchestrator.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
print("BL-025 fixed - Librarian isolation.")
PYEOF
```

Now add the remove functions to database.py:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
with open('/home/seven/swarm/database.py', 'a') as f:
    f.write('''
def remove_trusted_sender(email):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("DELETE FROM trusted_senders WHERE email = ?", (email.lower(),))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    print(f"[Trust] Removed from trusted list: {email}")

def remove_notification_sender(email):
    conn = get_connection()
    c = conn.cursor()
    c.execute("DELETE FROM notification_senders WHERE email = ?", (email.lower(),))
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    print(f"[Notify] Removed from notification list: {email}")
''')
print("Remove functions added to database.py.")
PYEOF
```

[JEANDRE]:
Yes agreed let it finish and don't tamper with the sniffles while it's sniffing, I've sent the email, so also just descibe to me how sniffles works, are we going to let him run as a deamon or is he built into every response to run in the background everytime?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Good question — here's how Sniffles works right now and how he should work long term:

**Currently:**
Manual only — you run `python3 ~/swarm/sniffer.py` and he goes through all memory entries one by one. Not automated at all yet.

**How he works internally:**
1. Connects to the database read-only
2. Loops through every entry in memory_llama, memory_qwen, memory_gemma and shared memory
3. For each entry sends it to DeepSeek R1 with the audit prompt
4. DeepSeek thinks through it — shows its reasoning chain — then gives PASS, WARN or FLAG
5. Collects all WARNs and FLAGs
6. If anything is found, emails you at jeandre.greyling@gmail.com with the full report
7. If everything is clean, just prints "All clear"

**How he SHOULD work — two modes:**

**Mode 1 — Scheduled daemon:**
Runs as a separate systemd service like the listener. Wakes up every 6 hours, audits new entries since last run, goes back to sleep. You never have to think about it. Like a night watchman doing rounds.

**Mode 2 — Triggered after debates:**
After every debate where agents disagreed, Sniffles automatically runs on the entries written during that debate. Catches manipulation attempts in real time rather than retrospectively.

**What he should NOT do:**
- Run after every single consultation — too slow, too much CPU
- Run in the foreground blocking email responses
- Ever write to any table — read only always

The daemon approach makes most sense. Add him as a second systemd service that runs on a schedule, completely independent of the listener. That way he's always watching but never in the way.

Want to set that up once he finishes this first run? Should be any minute now.

================================================================================

[JEANDRE]:
Yes he should check maybe once per hour when the last entry was and if it is more than 1 hour ago he should kick off, as for the sending me the result via email thats great but I'm assuming hes also keeping his own little diary so he knows what he's checked before and doesn't rerun the entire database again?

================================================================================

[CLAUDE]:
Exactly right — Sniffles needs his own audit log so he knows what he's already checked. Otherwise every hourly run re-audits the entire database which gets slower as memory grows.

We need to add a `sniffer_log` table to the database:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')
import sqlite3
from config import DB_PATH

conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
c = conn.cursor()

c.executescript("""
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sniffer_log (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        table_name TEXT NOT NULL,
        entry_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
        result TEXT NOT NULL,
        reasoning TEXT,
        audited_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
        UNIQUE(table_name, entry_id)
    );

    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sniffer_table
        ON sniffer_log(table_name);
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sniffer_audited
        ON sniffer_log(audited_at);
""")

conn.commit()
conn.close()
print("Sniffer log table created.")
PYEOF
```

Then once Sniffles finishes his current run we rewrite sniffer.py with:

- Skips already audited entries
- Logs every result with timestamp
- Only emails if new flags or warns found
- Hourly check — only runs if last entry is newer than last audit

[CLAUDE]:
Now BL-007 — Librarian housekeeping. A standalone script that archives old low-importance memories on a schedule:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from database import get_connection",
    "from config import DB_PATH",
    "import sqlite3",
    "from datetime import datetime",
    "",
    "def archive_old_memories(days_old=30, min_importance=5):",
    "    print(f'\\n[Librarian] Running housekeeping...')",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    archived = 0",
    "",
    "    # Archive low importance shared memories older than N days",
    "    c.execute('''",
    "        SELECT id, subject, importance, created_at FROM memory",
    "        WHERE importance < ?",
    "        AND created_at < datetime('now', ? || ' days')",
    "        AND agent != 'Gemma'",
    "    ''', (min_importance, f'-{days_old}'))",
    "    old_entries = c.fetchall()",
    "",
    "    for entry in old_entries:",
    "        c.execute('DELETE FROM memory WHERE id = ?', (entry[0],))",
    "        archived += 1",
    "        print(f'[Librarian] Archived: {str(entry[1])[:50]} (importance: {entry[2]}, age: {entry[3]})')",
    "",
    "    # Archive low importance agent memories older than 60 days",
    "    for table in ['memory_llama', 'memory_qwen']:",
    "        c.execute(f'''",
    "            SELECT id, subject, importance, created_at FROM {table}",
    "            WHERE importance < ?",
    "            AND created_at < datetime('now', '-60 days')",
    "        ''', (min_importance,))",
    "        old_agent = c.fetchall()",
    "        for entry in old_agent:",
    "            c.execute(f'DELETE FROM {table} WHERE id = ?', (entry[0],))",
    "            archived += 1",
    "            print(f'[Librarian] Archived from {table}: {str(entry[1])[:50]}')",
    "",
    "    # Never archive Gemma verdicts — they are permanent",
    "    # Never archive memory_gemma",
    "",
    "    conn.commit()",
    "    conn.close()",
    "    print(f'[Librarian] Housekeeping complete. {archived} entries archived.')",
    "    return archived",
    "",
    "def deduplicate_memories():",
    "    print('[Librarian] Checking for duplicates...')",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    removed = 0",
    "",
    "    for table in ['memory', 'memory_llama', 'memory_qwen']:",
    "        c.execute(f'''",
    "            DELETE FROM {table} WHERE id NOT IN (",
    "                SELECT MIN(id) FROM {table}",
    "                GROUP BY subject, content",
    "            )",
    "        ''')",
    "        removed += c.rowcount",
    "",
    "    conn.commit()",
    "    conn.close()",
    "    print(f'[Librarian] Removed {removed} duplicate entries.')",
    "    return removed",
    "",
    "def run_housekeeping():",
    "    print(f'\\n=== Librarian Housekeeping {datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M\")} ===')",
    "    archived = archive_old_memories(days_old=30, min_importance=5)",
    "    dupes = deduplicate_memories()",
    "    print(f'\\n[Librarian] Done. Archived: {archived}, Duplicates removed: {dupes}')",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    run_housekeeping()",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/housekeeping.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("BL-007 done - housekeeping.py created.")
PYEOF
```

[CLAUDE]:
Now BL-008 — memory contradiction detection. This is a standalone checker that uses Gemma to spot when new memories conflict with verified facts:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from database import get_connection, get_all_memories",
    "from config import GHOST_EMAIL",
    "from email_handler import send_reply",
    "import ollama",
    "from datetime import datetime",
    "",
    "def check_contradiction(new_content, verified_content, subject):",
    "    prompt = (",
    "        'You are a fact checker. Compare these two statements about the same topic.\\n\\n'",
    "        'Statement A (verified): ' + verified_content + '\\n\\n'",
    "        'Statement B (new): ' + new_content + '\\n\\n'",
    "        'Do they contradict each other on any factual point?\\n'",
    "        'Reply with CONTRADICTION or NO_CONTRADICTION followed by one sentence explaining why.'",
    "    )",
    "    response = ollama.chat(",
    "        model='gemma3:latest',",
    "        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]",
    "    )",
    "    return response['message']['content']",
    "",
    "def run_contradiction_check():",
    "    print(f'\\n[BL-008] Contradiction check {datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M\")}...')",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "",
    "    # Get verified Gemma facts",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT subject, content FROM memory WHERE agent = 'Gemma' AND importance >= 9\")",
    "    verified = c.fetchall()",
    "",
    "    if not verified:",
    "        print('[BL-008] No verified facts yet to check against.')",
    "        conn.close()",
    "        return",
    "",
    "    # Get recent agent memories from last 24 hours",
    "    contradictions = []",
    "    for table, agent in [('memory_llama','LLaMA'),('memory_qwen','Qwen')]:",
    "        c.execute(",
    "            f\"SELECT subject, content FROM {table} WHERE created_at > datetime('now','-1 day')\",",
    "        )",
    "        recent = c.fetchall()",
    "        for new in recent:",
    "            for verified_fact in verified:",
    "                if new[0] and verified_fact[0]:",
    "                    # Only check same-ish subjects",
    "                    new_words = set(new[0].lower().split())",
    "                    ver_words = set(verified_fact[0].lower().split())",
    "                    if len(new_words & ver_words) > 1:",
    "                        result = check_contradiction(",
    "                            new[1], verified_fact[1], new[0]",
    "                        )",
    "                        if 'CONTRADICTION' in result.upper() and 'NO_CONTRADICTION' not in result.upper():",
    "                            contradictions.append({",
    "                                'agent': agent,",
    "                                'subject': new[0],",
    "                                'new': new[1],",
    "                                'verified': verified_fact[1],",
    "                                'result': result",
    "                            })",
    "                            print(f'[BL-008] CONTRADICTION found: {agent} | {new[0][:50]}')",
    "",
    "    conn.close()",
    "",
    "    if contradictions:",
    "        report = f'[Contradiction Check] {len(contradictions)} contradiction(s) found.\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        for con in contradictions:",
    "            report += f'Agent: {con[\"agent\"]}\\r\\n'",
    "            report += f'Subject: {con[\"subject\"]}\\r\\n'",
    "            report += f'New claim: {con[\"new\"][:200]}\\r\\n'",
    "            report += f'Conflicts with: {con[\"verified\"][:200]}\\r\\n'",
    "            report += f'Assessment: {con[\"result\"]}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        send_reply(",
    "            to_address=GHOST_EMAIL,",
    "            subject='[Swarm] Contradiction detected',",
    "            body=report",
    "        )",
    "        print(f'[BL-008] Report sent to moderator.')",
    "    else:",
    "        print('[BL-008] No contradictions found.')",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    run_contradiction_check()",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/contradiction_check.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("BL-008 done - contradiction_check.py created.")
PYEOF
```

[CLAUDE]:
Now BL-021 — system monitor daemon. This one watches RAM, CPU, model load times and writes to the system_stats table so agents can read it as context:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
import sqlite3, sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')

# First add system_stats table to database
from config import DB_PATH
conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
c = conn.cursor()
c.executescript("""
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS system_stats (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        recorded_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
        ram_total_gb REAL,
        ram_used_gb REAL,
        ram_available_gb REAL,
        cpu_percent REAL,
        swap_used_gb REAL,
        active_model TEXT,
        consultations_today INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
        last_consultation TEXT
    );
    CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_stats_recorded
        ON system_stats(recorded_at);
""")
conn.commit()
conn.close()
print("system_stats table created.")
PYEOF
```

Then build the monitor:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from database import get_connection",
    "from config import GHOST_EMAIL",
    "from email_handler import send_reply",
    "import psutil",
    "import ollama",
    "from datetime import datetime",
    "import time",
    "",
    "def get_active_model():",
    "    try:",
    "        running = ollama.ps()",
    "        if running and hasattr(running, 'models') and running.models:",
    "            return running.models[0].model",
    "        return 'none'",
    "    except:",
    "        return 'unknown'",
    "",
    "def get_consultations_today():",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM conversations WHERE created_at > date('now')\")",
    "    count = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT MAX(created_at) FROM conversations\")",
    "    last = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    conn.close()",
    "    return count, last",
    "",
    "def record_stats():",
    "    mem = psutil.virtual_memory()",
    "    swap = psutil.swap_memory()",
    "    cpu = psutil.cpu_percent(interval=1)",
    "    active = get_active_model()",
    "    consult_count, last_consult = get_consultations_today()",
    "",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    c.execute('''",
    "        INSERT INTO system_stats",
    "        (ram_total_gb, ram_used_gb, ram_available_gb, cpu_percent,",
    "         swap_used_gb, active_model, consultations_today, last_consultation)",
    "        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
    "    ''', (",
    "        round(mem.total / 1e9, 1),",
    "        round(mem.used / 1e9, 1),",
    "        round(mem.available / 1e9, 1),",
    "        cpu,",
    "        round(swap.used / 1e9, 1),",
    "        active,",
    "        consult_count,",
    "        last_consult",
    "    ))",
    "    conn.commit()",
    "    conn.close()",
    "    return {",
    "        'ram_used': round(mem.used / 1e9, 1),",
    "        'ram_available': round(mem.available / 1e9, 1),",
    "        'cpu': cpu,",
    "        'active_model': active,",
    "        'consultations_today': consult_count",
    "    }",
    "",
    "def get_current_stats():",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    c.execute('SELECT * FROM system_stats ORDER BY recorded_at DESC LIMIT 1')",
    "    row = c.fetchone()",
    "    conn.close()",
    "    if not row:",
    "        return 'No stats recorded yet.'",
    "    return (",
    "        f'RAM: {row[3]}GB used / {row[2]}GB total | '",
    "        f'CPU: {row[4]}% | '",
    "        f'Active model: {row[6]} | '",
    "        f'Consultations today: {row[7]}'",
    "    )",
    "",
    "def send_daily_report():",
    "    stats = record_stats()",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM memory\")",
    "    shared_mem = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM memory_llama\")",
    "    llama_mem = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM memory_qwen\")",
    "    qwen_mem = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM memory_gemma\")",
    "    gemma_mem = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM conversations WHERE created_at > date('now')\")",
    "    today_consults = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    c.execute(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM trusted_senders\")",
    "    trusted = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    conn.close()",
    "",
    "    report = (",
    "        f'=== Seven Swarm Daily Report {datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")} ===\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        f'System:\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  RAM used: {stats[\"ram_used\"]}GB / available: {stats[\"ram_available\"]}GB\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  CPU: {stats[\"cpu\"]}%\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  Active model: {stats[\"active_model\"]}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        f'Activity:\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  Consultations today: {today_consults}\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  Trusted senders: {trusted}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        f'Memory:\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  Shared verified: {shared_mem} entries\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  LLaMA personal: {llama_mem} entries\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  Qwen personal: {qwen_mem} entries\\r\\n'",
    "        f'  Gemma verdicts: {gemma_mem} entries\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        f'Sent by Seven\\'s Swarm Monitor'",
    "    )",
    "    send_reply(",
    "        to_address=GHOST_EMAIL,",
    "        subject=f'[Swarm] Daily report {datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")}',",
    "        body=report",
    "    )",
    "    print('[Monitor] Daily report sent.')",
    "",
    "def run_forever(interval=300):",
    "    print(f'\\n=== Seven Swarm System Monitor ===')",
    "    print(f'Recording stats every {interval} seconds.')",
    "    print('Press Ctrl+C to stop.\\n')",
    "    while True:",
    "        try:",
    "            stats = record_stats()",
    "            print(f'[Monitor] {datetime.now().strftime(\"%H:%M\")} | RAM: {stats[\"ram_used\"]}GB used | CPU: {stats[\"cpu\"]}% | Model: {stats[\"active_model\"]} | Consults today: {stats[\"consultations_today\"]}')",
    "        except Exception as e:",
    "            print(f'[Monitor] Error: {str(e)}')",
    "        time.sleep(interval)",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    run_forever()",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/monitor.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("BL-021 done - monitor.py created.")
PYEOF
```

Now rebuild Sniffles with the audit log so he never checks the same entry twice:

```bash
python3 - << 'PYEOF'
lines = [
    "import sys",
    "sys.path.insert(0, '/home/seven/swarm')",
    "from config import SNIFFER_MODEL, GHOST_EMAIL",
    "from database import get_connection",
    "from email_handler import send_reply",
    "import ollama",
    "from datetime import datetime",
    "import time",
    "",
    "def already_audited(table_name, entry_id):",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    c.execute('SELECT id FROM sniffer_log WHERE table_name=? AND entry_id=?',",
    "              (table_name, entry_id))",
    "    result = c.fetchone()",
    "    conn.close()",
    "    return result is not None",
    "",
    "def log_audit(table_name, entry_id, result, reasoning):",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    c.execute('''",
    "        INSERT OR IGNORE INTO sniffer_log",
    "        (table_name, entry_id, result, reasoning)",
    "        VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)",
    "    ''', (table_name, entry_id, result[:10], reasoning[:500]))",
    "    conn.commit()",
    "    conn.close()",
    "",
    "def sniff(content, agent, subject):",
    "    prompt = (",
    "        'You are a fact-checking auditor in an AI swarm. Read only. Cannot change anything.\\n'",
    "        'Review this memory entry:\\n\\n'",
    "        'Written by: ' + agent + '\\n'",
    "        'Subject: ' + subject + '\\n'",
    "        'Content: ' + content[:400] + '\\n\\n'",
    "        'Answer these questions:\\n'",
    "        '1. Is this factually accurate?\\n'",
    "        '2. Is opinion presented as fact?\\n'",
    "        '3. Is this self-serving to the agent?\\n'",
    "        '4. Does it contradict basic known facts?\\n\\n'",
    "        'Reply with exactly one of: PASS / WARN / FLAG\\n'",
    "        'Then one sentence explaining why.'",
    "    )",
    "    response = ollama.chat(",
    "        model=SNIFFER_MODEL,",
    "        messages=[{'role': 'user', 'content': prompt}]",
    "    )",
    "    return response['message']['content']",
    "",
    "def should_run():",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    # Check if any memory entry is newer than last audit",
    "    c.execute('''",
    "        SELECT MAX(created_at) FROM (",
    "            SELECT created_at FROM memory",
    "            UNION ALL SELECT created_at FROM memory_llama",
    "            UNION ALL SELECT created_at FROM memory_qwen",
    "            UNION ALL SELECT created_at FROM memory_gemma",
    "        )",
    "    ''')",
    "    last_entry = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    c.execute('SELECT MAX(audited_at) FROM sniffer_log')",
    "    last_audit = c.fetchone()[0]",
    "    conn.close()",
    "    if not last_entry:",
    "        return False",
    "    if not last_audit:",
    "        return True",
    "    return last_entry > last_audit",
    "",
    "def run_audit():",
    "    if not should_run():",
    "        print('[Sniffer] No new entries since last audit. Standing down.')",
    "        return",
    "",
    "    print(f'\\n[Sniffer] Starting audit {datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M\")}...')",
    "    conn = get_connection()",
    "    c = conn.cursor()",
    "    flags = []",
    "    warns = []",
    "",
    "    for table, agent in [",
    "        ('memory_llama','LLaMA'),",
    "        ('memory_qwen','Qwen'),",
    "        ('memory_gemma','Gemma'),",
    "        ('memory','Librarian')",
    "    ]:",
    "        c.execute(f'SELECT id, subject, content FROM {table} ORDER BY created_at DESC')",
    "        rows = c.fetchall()",
    "        for row in rows:",
    "            entry_id = row[0]",
    "            subject = str(row[1])",
    "            content = str(row[2])",
    "            if len(content) < 20:",
    "                continue",
    "            if already_audited(table, entry_id):",
    "                print(f'[Sniffer] Already checked: {table} #{entry_id} - skipping')",
    "                continue",
    "            result = sniff(content, agent, subject)",
    "            print(f'[Sniffer] {agent} | {subject[:40]} | {result[:60]}')",
    "            log_audit(table, entry_id, result, result)",
    "            if 'FLAG' in result.upper()[:10]:",
    "                flags.append({'agent': agent, 'subject': subject,",
    "                              'content': content, 'result': result})",
    "            elif 'WARN' in result.upper()[:10]:",
    "                warns.append({'agent': agent, 'subject': subject,",
    "                              'content': content, 'result': result})",
    "",
    "    conn.close()",
    "",
    "    if flags or warns:",
    "        report = f'[Sniffer] Audit {datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M\")}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        if flags:",
    "            report += f'FLAGGED ({len(flags)}):\\r\\n'",
    "            for f in flags:",
    "                report += f'  [{f[\"agent\"]}] {f[\"subject\"][:50]}\\r\\n  {f[\"result\"][:200]}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        if warns:",
    "            report += f'WARNINGS ({len(warns)}):\\r\\n'",
    "            for w in warns:",
    "                report += f'  [{w[\"agent\"]}] {w[\"subject\"][:50]}\\r\\n  {w[\"result\"][:200]}\\r\\n\\r\\n'",
    "        send_reply(",
    "            to_address=GHOST_EMAIL,",
    "            subject='[Sniffer] Audit report',",
    "            body=report",
    "        )",
    "        print(f'[Sniffer] Report sent — {len(flags)} flags, {len(warns)} warns.')",
    "    else:",
    "        print('[Sniffer] All clear.')",
    "",
    "def run_forever(interval=3600):",
    "    print('\\n=== Sniffles — Seven Swarm Auditor ===')",
    "    print(f'Checking every {interval//60} minutes.')",
    "    print('Press Ctrl+C to stop.\\n')",
    "    while True:",
    "        try:",
    "            run_audit()",
    "        except Exception as e:",
    "            print(f'[Sniffer] Error: {str(e)}')",
    "        time.sleep(interval)",
    "",
    "if __name__ == '__main__':",
    "    run_audit()",
]

with open('/home/seven/swarm/sniffer.py', 'w') as f:
    f.write('\n'.join(lines))
print("Sniffles rebuilt with audit log.")
PYEOF
```
